What Is Sex? —— Alenka Zupančič

发表于2026-10-20更新于2026-11-26
字数总计:90.7k阅读时长:377分钟 中国
笔记精神分析

What Is Sex? —— Alenka Zupančič

Ivoripuion2026-10-202026-11-26

丛书前言

A short circuit occurs when there is a faulty connection in the network—faulty, of course, from the standpoint of the network’s smooth functioning. Is not the shock of short-circuiting, therefore, one of the best metaphors for a critical reading? Is not one of the most effective critical procedures to cross wires that do not usually touch: to take a major classic (text, author, notion) and read it in a short-circuiting way, through the lens of a “minor” author, text, or conceptual apparatus (“minor” should be understood here in Deleuze’s sense: not “of lesser quality,” but marginalized, disavowed by the hegemonic ideology, or dealing with a “lower,” less dignified topic)? If the minor reference is well chosen, such a procedure can lead to insights which completely shatter and undermine our common perceptions. This is what Marx, among others, did with philosophy and religion (short-circuiting philosophical speculation through the lens of political economy, that is to say, economic speculation); this is what Freud and Nietzsche did with morality (short-circuiting the highest ethical notions through the lens of the unconscious libidinal economy). What such a reading achieves is not a simple “desublimation,” a reduction of the higher intellectual content to its lower economic or libidinal cause; the aim of such an approach is, rather, the inherent decentering of the interpreted text, which brings to light its “unthought,” its disavowed presuppositions and consequences.

短路发生在网络中出现错误连接的时候——当然，所谓“错误”，是从网络顺畅运行的角度看的。因此，短路的冲击难道不是批判性阅读最好的隐喻之一吗？最有效的批判操作之一，难道不正是把通常并不相接的线路交叉接通：拿一部重要经典（文本、作者、概念），透过一个“次要”作者、文本或概念装置的透镜，对它作短路式阅读？（这里的“次要”要按德勒兹的意思理解：不是“质量较低”，而是被边缘化、被霸权意识形态否认，或处理“较低”、不那么体面的话题。）如果这个次要参照选得准，这种操作就能产生洞见，彻底击碎并瓦解我们的常识感知。马克思对哲学和宗教做的就是这样：用政治经济学的透镜——也就是经济投机/思辨——去短路哲学思辨；弗洛伊德和尼采对道德做的也是如此：用无意识力比多经济的透镜去短路最高的伦理观念。这样的阅读所达到的，不是简单的“去升华”，不是把较高的智识内容还原为较低的经济或力比多原因；相反，这种进路的目标，是被解释文本的内在去中心化，让它暴露出自身的“未思”、它被否认的前提与后果。

And this is what “Short Circuits” wants to do, again and again. The underlying premise of the series is that Lacanian psychoanalysis is a privileged instrument of such an approach, whose purpose is to illuminate a standard text or ideological formation, making it readable in a totally new way—the long history of Lacanian interventions in philosophy, religion, the arts (from the visual arts to the cinema, music, and literature), ideology, and politics justifies this premise. This, then, is not a new series of books on psychoanalysis, but a series of “connections in the Freudian field”—of short Lacanian interventions in art, philosophy, theology, and ideology.

而这正是“短路”想做、并且会一次又一次去做的事。该丛书的基本前提是：拉康派精神分析是这种路径的一个优先工具；它的目的，是照亮一部标准文本或一种意识形态构造，让它以全新的方式变得可读——拉康派在哲学、宗教、艺术（从视觉艺术到电影、音乐和文学）、意识形态和政治中的长期介入史，足以证明这一前提。因此，这不是一套关于精神分析的新书，而是一套“弗洛伊德场域中的连接”——即拉康派对艺术、哲学、神学和意识形态所作的一系列简短介入。

“Short Circuits” intends to revive a practice of reading which confronts a classic text, author, or notion with its own hidden presuppositions, and thus reveals its disavowed truth. The basic criterion for the texts that will be published is that they effectuate such a theoretical short circuit. After reading a book in this series, the reader should not simply have learned something new: the point is, rather, to make him or her aware of another—disturbing—side of something he or she knew all the time.

——Slavoj Žižek

“短路”丛书想复兴一种阅读实践：让经典文本、作者或概念面对它自身隐藏的预设，从而揭示出它被否认的真理。将要出版的文本，基本标准就是它们能实现这样一种理论短路。读完本丛书中的一本书，读者不该只是学到某种新东西；关键更在于，让他/她意识到自己一直知道的东西还有另一面——令人不安的一面。

——斯拉沃热·齐泽克

Introduction

“For the moment, I am not fucking, I am talking to you. Well! I can have exactly the same satisfaction as if I were fucking.” This is the example that Lacan comes up with to illustrate the claim that sublimation is satisfaction of the drive, without repression. We usually tend to think of sublimation in terms of a substitute satisfaction: instead of “fucking,” I engage in talking (writing, painting, praying …)—this way I get another kind of satisfaction to replace the “missing” one. Sublimations are substitute satisfactions for a missing sexual satisfaction. The point that Lacanian psychoanalysis makes, however, is more paradoxical: the activity is different, yet the satisfaction is exactly the same. In other words, the point is not to explain the satisfaction in talking by referring to its “sexual origin.” The point is that the satisfaction in talking is itself “sexual.” And this is precisely what forces us to open the question of the very nature and status of sexuality in a radical way. Marx famously wrote that “human anatomy contains the key to the anatomy of the ape” (and not, perhaps, the other way around). In a similar way, we should insist that the satisfaction in talking contains a key to sexual satisfaction (and not the other way around), or simply a key to sexuality and its inherent contradictions. Hence the simple (and yet the most difficult) question that orients this book: What is sex? The way in which I propose to approach the question of sexuality is to consider it a properly philosophical problem of psychoanalysis—with everything that resonates with this term, starting with ontology, logic, and the theory of the subject.

“眼下，我并不是在操，我是在跟你们说话。好吧！我可以得到和正在操时一模一样的满足。”这就是拉康举的例子，用来说明：升华就是冲动的满足，而且无需压抑。我们通常倾向于把升华理解为一种替代性满足：不去“操”，而是去说话（写作、绘画、祈祷……）——这样我就得到另一种满足，来替代那个“缺失的”满足。升华是对缺失的性满足的替代性满足。然而，拉康派精神分析的要点更悖论：活动不同，满足却完全一样。换言之，要点并不是借助其“性起源”来解释说话中的满足。要点在于，说话中的满足本身就是“性的”。而这恰恰迫使我们从根本上重新打开性的本性与地位这一问题。马克思有句名言：“人体解剖包含着猴体解剖的钥匙”（或许并非反过来）。类似地，我们也应坚持：说话中的满足包含着理解性满足的钥匙（而不是反过来），或者干脆说，包含着理解性及其内在矛盾的钥匙。因此，本书所围绕的简单（也最难）的问题就是：性是什么？我建议这样接近性问题：把它当作精神分析的一个真正哲学问题——连同这个说法所唤起的一切，从本体论、逻辑和主体理论开始。

note：这里要注意的是，升华并没有替换冲动对象（一直围绕者对象a），而是把冲动对象崇高化，也就是说把一个客体升华为到了对象a的位置上。

Psychoanalysis (in its Freudo-Lacanian lineage) has been, among other things, a very powerful conceptual invention, with direct and significant resonances in philosophy. The encounter between philosophy and psychoanalysis has turned out to be one of the most productive construction sites in contemporary philosophy. It has produced some impressive new and original readings of classical philosophers and of classical philosophical concepts (such as subject, object, truth, representation, real), and opened a genuinely new vein in contemporary philosophy. At the moment when philosophy itself was just about ready to abandon some of its classical notions as belonging to its own metaphysical past, from which it was eager to escape, along came Lacan, and taught us an invaluable lesson: it is not these notions themselves that are problematic; what is problematic (in some ways of doing philosophy) is the disavowal or effacement of the inherent contradiction (or antagonism) they all imply, and are part of. That is why, by simply abandoning these notions, we are abandoning the battlefield, rather than winning any significant battles. In a similar, albeit not symmetrical, way, psychoanalysis (also in its clinical context) has gained a great deal by hanging onto and operating with philosophical concepts, and by playing a part in philosophical debates. For in this way it remained involved in the general intellectual landscape, its struggles and antagonisms, which it has itself brought to light, rather than enclosing itself in a safely circumscribed, specialized field of expertise and practice. And this was precisely the divide that Lacan kept pointing out, and which has been at the heart of his quarrel with (that is, his expulsion from) the International Psychoanalytic Association: the divide between psychoanalysis as a recognized therapeutic practice, appropriately confined to, or allocated, its field/feud, and what seemed to be Lacan’s intellectual (and practical) extravagances which were, quite literally, all over the place (philosophy, science, literature …). It was here, and not simply in the battle between different psychoanalytic orientations, that Lacan situated the real divide. Apart from the famous short sessions, “intellectualization” was the key word and the key insult aimed at what he was doing in his “teaching” (which itself took place outside of psychoanalytic practice, and had a universal destination)—an insult aimed by analysts whom Lacan did not hesitate to insult back, calling them “orthopedists of the unconscious” and “guarantors of the bourgeois dream.” The alleged “intellectualization” was not due simply to Lacan’s persona (his own intelligence, erudition, ambition), but to what he recognized to be at the very core of Freud’s discovery, causing its main scandal. “The unconscious thinks” is how Lacan liked to formulate the gist of that discovery. Ingenious dreams, slips of the tongue, jokes, as well as many (other) highly spiritual forms and creations, are all manifestations of the work of the unconscious. … There is nothing simply irrational about the unconscious. Lacan also liked to point out how the biggest scandal provoked by the Freudian notion of sexuality (as related to the unconscious) was not its alleged dirtiness, but the fact “that it was so ‘intellectual.’ It was in this respect that it showed itself to be the worthy stooge of all those terrorists whose plots were going to ruin society” (Lacan 2006b, 435). In this precise sense, to say that the satisfaction in talking (or in any kind of intellectual activity) is “sexual” is not simply about abasement of intellectual activities, it is at least as much about elevating sexuality to a surprisingly intellectual activity…

精神分析（在其弗洛伊德—拉康谱系中）一直是一项极强的概念发明，并且在哲学中产生了直接而重要的回响。哲学与精神分析的相遇，已成为当代哲学中最富生产性的建构现场之一。它带来了一些令人印象深刻、新颖而原创的解读，既针对古典哲学家，也针对古典哲学概念（如主体、客体、真理、表象、实在界），并开启了一条真正新的当代哲学脉络。就在哲学本身正准备把某些古典概念当作属于自己形而上学过去、并急于摆脱的东西而抛弃时，拉康登场了，给我们上了极宝贵的一课：问题不在于这些概念本身；问题在于（在某些做哲学的方式中）对它们全都隐含、并且本身也参与其中的内在矛盾（或对抗）的否认或抹除。正因如此，仅仅放弃这些概念，我们就是在放弃战场，而不是赢得任何重要战斗。以类似但并不对称的方式，精神分析（在其临床语境中也是如此）通过抓住并运用哲学概念、通过在哲学论争中发挥作用，而获益良多。因为这样一来，它仍然卷入一般的思想景观、其中的斗争与对抗——而这些正是它自己揭示出来的——而不是把自己封闭在一个安全划定的、专业化的知识与实践领域里。而这也正是拉康不断指出的分裂，也是他与国际精神分析协会争执（也就是他被该协会逐出）的核心所在：一边是作为被承认的治疗实践、被恰当地限定于或分配给它自己的领域/纷争的精神分析；另一边则是拉康那些显得像智识（和实践）上越界挥霍的东西——它们简直无处不在（哲学、科学、文学……）。拉康把真正的分裂定位在这里，而不只是定位在不同精神分析取向之间的斗争里。除了著名的短会谈之外，“理智化”是分析家们针对拉康在其“教学”中所做之事的关键词和关键侮辱（教学本身发生在精神分析实践之外，并具有普遍指向）；拉康毫不犹豫地回敬这些分析家，称他们为“无意识的矫正师”和“资产阶级梦想的担保人”。所谓“理智化”并不只是源于拉康的个人形象（他自己的智力、博学、抱负），而是源于他认作弗洛伊德发现最核心之处、并造成其主要丑闻的东西。拉康喜欢用“无意识会思考”来概括这一发现的要点。精巧的梦、口误、笑话，以及许多（别的）高度精神性的形式和创造，都是无意识工作的显现。……无意识并非单纯非理性的。拉康也喜欢指出，弗洛伊德式性概念（作为与无意识相关的概念）所引发的最大的丑闻，并不是它据说很肮脏，而是这样一个事实：“它竟然如此‘智识化’。”正是在这一点上，它显现为所有那些阴谋要毁掉社会的恐怖分子的称职跟班（Lacan 2006b, 435）。在这个确切意义上，说言说（或任何智识活动）中的满足是“性的”，并不只是贬低智识活动，至少同样是在把性提升为一种令人惊讶的智识活动。

There is thus little doubt about where Lacan situated the most important divide and struggle in psychoanalysis: “I would like to say, to those who are listening to me, how they could recognize bad psychoanalysts: by the word they use to depreciate all research on technique and theory that furthers the Freudian experience in its authentic dimension. That word is ‘intellectualization’ …” (Lacan 2006b, 435).

因此，拉康把精神分析中最重要的分界与斗争放在哪里，几乎没有疑问：“我想对正在听我讲的人说：他们怎样认出糟糕的分析家？就看他们用哪个词来贬低一切在技术和理论上推进弗洛伊德经验真正维度的研究。这个词就是‘理智化’……”（Lacan 2006b, 435）

If, however, the encounter between psychoanalysis and philosophy has proved to be a most inspiring and fruitful construction site for both, it seems that avoiding this site has recently become more and more of the mot d’ordre (or fashion) in both fields. Philosophers have rediscovered pure philosophy, and particularly ontology; engaged as they are in producing new ontologies, they see little interest in what looks at best like a regional theory corresponding to a particular therapeutic practice. (Lacanian) psychoanalysts, on the other hand, are busy rediscovering the “experimental” (clinical) core of their concepts, which they sometimes like to present as their holy grail—the ultimate Real that they, and nobody else, are in touch with.

然而，如果说精神分析与哲学的相遇已经被证明是双方一个极富启发、成果丰硕的“建构工地”，那么近来，回避这个工地似乎越来越成为两个领域的口号（或者说时髦）。哲学家重新发现了纯粹哲学，尤其是本体论；他们正忙于生产新的本体论，因此对那个顶多看起来像是一种对应于某种特定治疗实践的局部理论的东西兴趣寥寥。另一方面，（拉康派）精神分析家则忙着重新发现其概念中的“实验性”（临床）内核；他们有时喜欢把这个内核呈现为自己的圣杯——终极的实在界，只有他们、没有别人能触及。

In this respect, this book goes—both methodologically and ideologically—against the grain of the “times we live in,” refusing to abandon the construction site in favor of more polished “conceptual products,” “services,” or “singular experiences.” The pages that follow grew out of a double conviction: first, that in psychoanalysis sex is above all a concept that formulates a persisting contradiction of reality. And, second, that this contradiction cannot be circumscribed or reduced to a secondary level (as a contradiction between already well-established entities/beings), but is—as a contradiction—involved in the very structuring of these entities, in their very being. In this precise sense, sex is of ontological relevance: not as an ultimate reality, but as an inherent twist, or stumbling block, of reality.

在这方面，这本书无论在方法论上还是在意识形态上，都与“我们所处的时代”格格不入：它拒绝放弃工地，转去追求更精致的“概念产品”“服务”或“独特经验”。下文源于双重信念：第一，在精神分析中，性首先是一个概念，它表述的是现实中一种持续存在的矛盾。第二，这一矛盾不能被限定或还原到次级层面（仿佛它只是已经稳固确立的实体/存在者之间的矛盾），而是——作为矛盾——参与到这些实体本身的结构化之中，参与到它们的存在本身之中。正是在这个精确意义上，性具有存在论相关性：它不是作为终极现实，而是作为现实内在的扭结，或者说现实的绊脚石。

The question of “Lacan and philosophy” is thus taken up and tackled here at the point where the stakes appear to be highest. Sex is the question usually left out in even the most friendly philosophical appropriations of Lacan and his concepts; and ontology is something that Lacan saw as related to the discourse of the master, playing on the homonymy between maître (master) and m’être (from being, être). Ontology implies “being at someone’s heel,” “being at someone’s beck and call” (Lacan 1999, 31).

因此，“拉康与哲学”这一问题，就在其利害显得最大之处被提出并加以处理。性，通常是连最友好的、对拉康及其概念的哲学挪用也会漏掉的问题；而本体论，则是拉康认为与主人话语相关的东西——这里玩弄的是 maître（主人）与 m’être（来自存在，être）之间的同音异义。本体论意味着“跟在某人脚后跟转”，意味着“听凭某人呼来喝去”（Lacan 1999, 31）。

And yet, or, more precisely: exactly because of this, it seems imperative to posit the question of “sex and ontology.” It is here, I claim, that the destiny of the encounter between philosophy and psychoanalysis is being decided and played out.

然而，或者更准确地说：恰恰因为这一点，提出“性与本体论”的问题似乎已经势在必行。我主张，正是在这里，哲学与精神分析之相遇的命运正在被决定，并正在上演。

As Louis Althusser argued in his powerful essay “On Marx and Freud,” one of the things Marxism and psychoanalysis have in common is that they are situated within the conflict that they theorize; they are themselves part of the very reality that they recognize as conflictual and antagonistic. In such a case the criterion of scientific objectivity is not a supposed neutrality, which is nothing other than a dissimulation (and hence the perpetuation) of the given antagonism, or of the point of real exploitation. In any social conflict, a “neutral” position is always and necessarily the position of the ruling class: it seems “neutral” because it has achieved the status of the dominant ideology, which always strikes us as self-evident. The criterion of objectivity in such a case is thus not neutrality, but the capacity of theory to occupy a singular, specific point of view within the situation. In this sense, the objectivity is linked here to the very capacity of being “partial” or “partisan.” As Althusser puts it: when dealing with a conflictual reality (which is the case for both Marxism and psychoanalysis) one cannot see everything from everywhere (on ne peut pas tout voir de partout); some positions dissimulate this conflict, and some reveal it. One can thus discover the essence of this conflictual reality only by occupying certain positions, and not others, in this very conflict (Althusser 1993, 229).

正如路易·阿尔都塞在《论马克思与弗洛伊德》这篇有力文章中所论证的，马克思主义和精神分析有一个共同点：它们都处在自己所理论化的冲突之中；它们本身就是它们所承认的那个冲突性、对抗性现实的一部分。在这种情况下，科学客观性的标准并不是一种被假定的中立——这种中立不过是对既定对抗、对实际剥削点的掩饰（并因此使之延续）。在任何社会冲突中，一个“中立”位置总是且必然是统治阶级的位置：它看起来“中立”，是因为它已经取得主导意识形态的地位，而主导意识形态总让我们觉得不言自明。因此，这种情形下的客观性标准不是中立，而是理论有能力在情境中占据一个单一而具体的视点。在这个意义上，客观性在这里联系于成为“片面”或“党派性”的能力。正如阿尔都塞所说：面对冲突性现实时（马克思主义和精神分析都是如此），人不可能从所有地方看到一切（on ne peut pas tout voir de partout）；有些位置掩饰这种冲突，有些位置揭示它。因此，只有在这场冲突中占据某些位置而非另一些位置，才能发现这种冲突性现实的本质（Althusser 1993, 229）。

note：$S_1->S_2$

What this book aims to show and argue is that sex, or the sexual, is precisely such a “position,” or point of view, in psychoanalysis. Not because of its (“dirty” or controversial) contents, but because of the singular form of contradiction that it forces us to see, to think, and to engage with.

本书想说明并论证的是：在精神分析中，性（sex），或性的维度（the sexual），恰恰就是这样一个“位置”，或者说一种视角。这样讲，不是因为它的内容（“脏”或有争议），而是因为它迫使我们去看、去想、去与之打交道的那种独特矛盾形式。

Although this may not be evident from its length, this book is the result of many years of conceptual work. This work has not been linear, but consisted of going forward and then coming back to the most difficult issues from different angles and perspectives, and finally of cutting out a lot of things—that is to say, words. Inevitably, several parts of this book have already appeared, over those years, as presentations of what has been ongoing research. In order to avoid any misunderstanding in this respect, however, I want to emphasize not only that this is not a collection of essays (which is quite obvious), but also that the already published parts constitute material which, quite simply, cannot pass for being the same in this book. Not only because it was significantly reshaped and modified at crucial conceptual points and junctures, but also because it is only in the present work that it becomes what it is, namely, part of developing a central, book-length argument.

尽管从篇幅上未必看得出来，这本书是多年概念工作的成果。这项工作不是线性的，而是往前走，又从不同角度和视角回到最困难的问题上，最后还砍掉了很多东西——也就是说，砍掉了很多词。多年来，本书的若干部分不可避免地已经发表过，作为对当时仍在进行中的研究的呈现。不过，为了避免在这方面的误解，我想强调：这不仅不是一本论文集（这一点相当明显），而且已经发表的部分所构成的材料，简单说来，也不能被当成与本书中相同的东西。这不仅因为它们在关键的概念节点和接合处经过了大幅重塑和修改，也因为只有在这部当前著作中，它才成为它真正所是的东西，即成为展开一个贯穿全书的中心论证的一部分。

Recently, Lorenzo Chiesa’s (The Not-Two) and Aaron Schuster’s (The Trouble with Pleasure) books were published in this same series—books the topics of which intersect with mine in more than one respect. If these outstanding works do not play a significant part in my discussion, the reason is very simple: for many years we have been working on these topics in our “parallel universes,” in friendly complicity, yet each pursuing his or her particular “obsession” and path into the topics. I thought it best to maintain the independence of our “parallel universes” here—a decision that should not be mistaken for a lack of acknowledgment of these significant works.

最近，Lorenzo Chiesa 的《非二》（The Not-Two）和 Aaron Schuster 的《快感的麻烦》（The Trouble with Pleasure）也在同一套丛书里出版了——这两本书的主题不止在一个方面与我的主题相交。如果这些杰出著作没有在我的讨论中占据重要位置，原因很简单：多年来，我们一直带着友好的默契，在各自的“平行宇宙”里处理这些主题，但每个人都沿着自己特定的“执念”和进入这些主题的路径往前走。我认为在这里最好保持我们“平行宇宙”的独立性——这一决定不应被误认为是对这些重要著作缺乏认可。

Chapter 1

It’s Getting Strange in Here …

Did Somebody Say Sex?

In John Huston’s movie Freud: The Secret Passion (1962), a very powerful scene depicts Freud presenting his theory of infantile sexuality to a large audience of educated men. His brief presentation is met with strong and loudly stated disapproval, interrupted by roaring after almost every sentence; several of the men leave the auditorium in protest, spitting on the floor next to Freud. At some point the chairman, trying to restore order, cries out: “Gentlemen, we are not in a political meeting!”

有人说“性”吗？

在约翰·休斯顿的电影《弗洛伊德：隐秘的激情》（1962）中，有一个极具冲击力的场景：弗洛伊德向一大群受过教育的男士讲述他的幼儿性欲理论。他简短的陈述遭到强烈而大声的反对；几乎每句话之后都响起一片吼声，把他的发言打断。好几个人为了表示抗议离开礼堂，还在弗洛伊德身旁的地上吐口水。某个时刻，主席试图恢复秩序，喊道：“先生们，我们不是在开政治集会！”

This is a very intriguing remark, pointing us straight in the right direction: that of a strange, surprising coincidence between politics and (the Freudian theory of) sexuality—and we will return to this coincidence in some of the chapters below. But let us first stop at the outrage provoked by the Freudian notion of sexuality (and especially of infantile sexuality). It is very easy, from today’s point of view, to miss what is going on here, and to simply attribute this kind of violent reaction to the Victorian morals of Freud’s time. Since then—we tend to think—we have learned to be very tolerant and to talk about sexuality quite openly; we know that “sexuality is nothing to be ashamed of,” and that it is even good for our (mental and physical) health. We also think that Freud’s discoveries about the determinant role of the “psychosexual” in our development have become largely integrated into the therapeutic practices of psychoanalytic lineage, albeit in somewhat diluted form. So it might come as a big surprise to learn that this is far from being the case. In 2009, Ofra Shalev and Hanoch Yerushalmi published a surprising study concerning the status of sexuality among contemporary therapists involved in psychoanalytic psychotherapy.1 The results of this study prompted Kaveh Zamanian to publish an article in which he sums up some results of this study:

With respect to the first theme, the therapists in the Shalev and Yerushalmi study tended to believe that sexuality serves as a defense against deeper and more difficult issues such as intimacy and self-identity. … In fact, sexual issues were viewed as an impediment to the goal of helping patients adjust to their surroundings and overall functioning. The third factor was a blurring of lines and utter confusion about intimacy versus sexuality. … They focused on sexual encounters rather than psychosexual aspects of development. Amazingly, two therapists expressed that “sexual issues should be treated by sexologists and not by psychotherapists.” Notably, most of the therapists in the study did not separate sexuality from intimate relationships and even confused love and sexuality. As an example, one therapist concluded that his patients “rarely talk about sexual issues” and that their discussion of romantic relationships “never [has] sexual connotations.” The fourth and final factor, and for me the most troubling, was the therapists’ tendency to avoid sexual issues out of discomfort. Several therapists in the study experienced discussion of sexual matters as a “form of hostility directed at them” and even felt “abused by their patients.” Again, shockingly, one therapist described one of her patients in the following manner: “It was as if he was thinking, this is therapy so I can talk about everything.” (Zamanian 2011, 38)

这是一个很耐人寻味的说法，它把我们直接指向正确的方向：政治与（弗洛伊德式）性欲理论之间那个奇怪而令人惊讶的巧合——下文若干章我们还会回到这个巧合。不过，先让我们停一下，看看弗洛伊德式性欲概念（尤其是婴儿性欲）所激起的愤怒。从今天的视角看，我们很容易错过这里真正发生的事，而只是把这种激烈反应简单归因于弗洛伊德时代的维多利亚道德。我们倾向于认为，从那以后我们已经学会非常宽容、相当公开地谈论性；我们知道“性没什么可羞耻的”，甚至有益于我们的（心理和身体）健康。我们也以为，弗洛伊德关于“心理性欲”在我们发展中具有决定作用的发现，大体上已被吸收进精神分析谱系的治疗实践，尽管形式有所稀释。因此，得知事实远非如此，可能会让人大吃一惊。2009年，Ofra Shalev和Hanoch Yerushalmi发表了一项令人意外的研究，考察当代从事精神分析取向心理治疗的治疗师如何看待性欲的地位。研究结果促使Kaveh Zamanian发表文章，总结这项研究的部分结果：

关于第一个主题，沙莱夫和耶鲁沙尔米研究中的治疗师倾向于认为，性欲充当一种防御，用来抵挡更深、更难的问题，比如亲密与自我身份。……事实上，性问题被视为一种阻碍，妨碍帮助病人适应周围环境和整体功能这一目标。第三个因素是亲密与性欲之间界限模糊、彻底混乱。……他们关注的是性遭遇，而不是发展中的心理性欲层面。令人惊讶的是，两位治疗师表示“性问题应由性学家处理，而不应由心理治疗师处理”。值得注意的是，研究中的大多数治疗师没有把性欲与亲密关系分开，甚至把爱与性欲混为一谈。例如，一位治疗师断定，他的病人“很少谈性问题”，而他们讨论恋爱关系时“从来［没有］性意涵”。第四个也是最后一个因素，对我而言最令人不安，是治疗师因不适而倾向于回避性问题。研究中若干治疗师把讨论性事务体验为“一种针对他们的敌意”，甚至感到“被病人侵犯”。同样令人震惊的是，一位治疗师这样描述她的一位病人：“就好像他在想，这是治疗，所以我可以什么都谈。”

ps：真有这样的治疗师的话，很难想象他是怎么能承担起心理治疗的道德责任的。。。

Considering Freud’s formulation of the one and only rule or imperative involved in psychoanalytic treatment, which is to say absolutely anything that comes to our mind, however unimportant or improper it may seem to us, this last line actually sounds like an excellent psychoanalytic joke. …

考虑到弗洛伊德对精神分析治疗中唯一一条规则或命令的表述——即把任何浮现在脑子里的东西都说出来，不管它在我们看来多么无关紧要、多么不得体——这最后一句听起来倒真像是一个绝妙的精神分析笑话……

If this is the state of things in “psychoanalytic psychotherapy,” we should not be surprised that the general Stimmung concerning sexuality, and particularly infantile sexuality, is not very different. This is in no way contradicted by the blatant media exposure and their abundant use of sexuality. There is no contradiction, because what is involved here is a systematic reduction of the notion of sexuality—its reduction to (different) “sexual practices” as constituting “sexual intercourse,” and surrounded by obligatory sexual innuendo, that is, by a vast ocean of sexual meanings. This is clearly how sexuality comes across for the therapists involved in Shalev’s and Yerushalmi’s study: as naughty things that one does or does not do, and that one can eventually harass one’s therapist with. If we understand it in this way, we can indeed agree with the claim that “sexuality serves as a defense against deeper and more difficult issues.” The ironic point is, of course, that for Freud sexuality was the “deeper and more difficult issue” behind different sexual practices, innuendos and meanings—that it was something inherently problematic, disruptive, rather than constructive, of identities. Sexual activity appeared to Freud as redoubled by its own inherent impasse and difficulty, and as such it called for serious, ontological inquiry. What was, and still is, disturbing about the Freudian discussion of sexuality is not simply sexuality itself—this kind of resistance, indignant at psychoanalytic “obsession with dirty matters,” was never the strongest one, and was soon marginalized by the progressive liberalism of morals. Much more disturbing was the thesis concerning the always problematic and (ontologically) uncertain character of sexuality itself. To the Victorians screaming “Sex is dirty,” Freud did not answer something like “No, it is not dirty, it is only natural,” but rather something like: “What is this ‘sex’ that you are talking about?”

如果“精神分析心理治疗”中的状况就是如此，那么，围绕性、尤其是幼儿性欲的一般 Stimmung（氛围/情调）与此没有太大不同，也就不足为奇。媒体对性的赤裸展示及其对性的大量使用，丝毫不与此矛盾。并不矛盾，因为这里发生的是对“性”这一观念的系统性缩减：把它缩减为各种（不同的）“性实践”，仿佛这些实践就构成了“性交”，并且其四周还环绕着强制性的性暗示，也就是一片浩瀚的性意义海洋。在 Shalev 与 Yerushalmi 的研究所涉及的治疗师那里，性显然就是这样被理解的：它是一些“淘气事”，人做或不做，最后还可以拿这些事去骚扰自己的治疗师。若这样理解，我们确实可以同意这个说法：“性欲充当了对更深、更困难问题的防御。”当然，讽刺之处在于：对弗洛伊德来说，性欲恰恰是各种性实践、性暗示和性意义背后的那个“更深、更困难的问题”——它内在地就是有问题的、扰乱性的，而不是建构身份认同的。性活动在弗洛伊德看来，被自身固有的僵局与困难所叠加，因此它要求一种严肃的存在论探究。弗洛伊德关于性的讨论之所以过去、现在都令人不安，并不只是性本身——那种对精神分析“迷恋脏事”感到愤怒的抵抗，从来不是最强的抵抗，而且很快就被道德上的进步自由主义边缘化了。更令人不安的，是关于性本身始终成问题、并且在（存在论上）不确定这一论点。面对高喊“性是肮脏的”的维多利亚人，弗洛伊德并没有回答“不，它不脏，它只是自然的”之类的话，而更像是回答：“你说的这个‘性’，究竟是什么？”

Psychoanalysis does, of course, start out from the vicissitudes of human beings, on which it focuses its investigations. What keeps it from becoming a kind of “psychologized” human-interest philosophy, however, is precisely its discovery of and insistence on the sexual as a factor of radical disorientation, a factor that keeps bringing into question all our representations of the entity called “human being.” This is why it would also be a big mistake to consider that, in Freudian theory, the sexual (in the sense of constitutively deviational partial drives) is the ultimate horizon of the animal called “human,” a kind of anchor point of irreducible humanity in psychoanalytic theory; on the contrary, it is the operator of the inhuman, the operator of dehumanization.

精神分析当然是从人的种种遭际和起伏出发的，并把研究聚焦于此。然而，使它不至于变成一种“心理学化”的人文关怀哲学的，恰恰是它发现并坚持：性（the sexual）是一个造成根本性失向的因素，一个不断质疑我们关于“人”这个实体的一切表象的因素。也正因此，如果认为在弗洛伊德理论中，性——即构成性偏离的部分冲动意义上的性——是那种叫作“人”的动物的最终视域，是精神分析理论中某种不可化约之人性的锚定点，那同样会是个大错；恰恰相反，它是非人的操作项，是去人性化的操作项。

And, incidentally, this is precisely what clears the ground for a possible theory of the subject (as developed by Lacan), in which the subject is something other than simply another name for an individual or a “person.” Moreover, it is precisely the sexual as the operator of the inhuman that opens the perspective of the universal in psychoanalysis, which it is often accused of missing because of its insistence on the sexual (including sexual difference). What Freud calls the sexual is thus not that which makes us human in any received meaning of this term, it is rather that which makes us subjects, or perhaps more precisely, it is coextensive with the emergence of the subject. And this “inhuman” aspect of sexuality is what Lacan emphasizes in various different ways, including his famous invention of the “lamella.”2

顺带说一句，正是这一点为一种可能的主体理论（如拉康所发展的）清出了场地：在这种理论里，主体并不只是个体或“人格”的另一个名称。更进一步说，正是作为非人之算子的性，打开了精神分析中普遍性的视角——精神分析常因坚持性（包括性差异）而被指责错失这种普遍性。因此，弗洛伊德所说的性，并不是按这个词任何通常含义使我们成为人的东西；它倒是使我们成为主体的东西，或者更准确地说，它与主体的出现同延。而性的这种“非人”面向，正是拉康以各种不同方式所强调的，包括他著名的“拉梅拉”发明。

note：性不是把人锚定在“人”里的东西，而是把主体从“人”的定义中剥离东西。

What is going on in the contemporary psychotherapeutic take on sexuality could thus be described as follows. In the first step, one diverges completely from the Freudian notion of sexuality, reducing it to a description of empirical features related to certain kinds of practice. Then, in a second step, one (dismissively) discovers that sexuality is exactly what one has reduced it to in the first step: namely, an overrated epiphenomenon. When one assumes, for example, that psychoanalysis claims that all our (neurotic) problems come from bad or insufficient sex, there is no more room left for—what? Psychoanalysis, precisely. This is the perhaps surprising point that Freud makes in his essay “Wild Psychoanalysis”; and it is exactly what the two seemingly opposite therapeutic perspectives (the one claiming that sex is the answer to everything, and the one dismissing sex as overrated) have in common: there is no room left for psychoanalysis in either of them. There is no room left for psychoanalysis, because psychoanalysis sees the impossibility of full sexual satisfaction—in the absence of all external obstacles—as a constitutive and integral part of unconscious sexuality as such.

因此，当代心理治疗对性的处理可以这样描述。第一步，它完全背离弗洛伊德的性概念，把性化约为对与某些实践类型相关的经验特征的描述。第二步，它（带着轻蔑地）发现，性恰恰就是它在第一步里被化约成的那个东西：一种被高估的副现象。比如，当人们假定精神分析主张我们所有（神经症性的）问题都来自糟糕或不足的性，那么还剩下空间给——什么呢？恰恰不再有空间留给精神分析。这也许正是弗洛伊德在《野蛮精神分析》（“Wild Psychoanalysis”）一文中提出的、令人意外的观点；它也恰恰是两个看似相反的治疗视角的共同点：一个声称性是一切问题的答案，另一个把性斥为被高估之物；两者都没有给精神分析留下空间。没有空间留给精神分析，是因为精神分析把“即便没有任何外部障碍，完全的性满足也不可能”这一点，看作无意识的性本身的一个构成性且不可分割的部分。

The same goes for the idea that for psychoanalysis, (almost) everything has sexual meaning, and that to understand this meaning is the key to psychological recovery. To see how this misses the point, we simply need to bear in mind how Freud was led to his theory of the sexual as constitutively problematic. He was not led to it simply by discovering and deciphering the sexual meaning “behind” symptoms and different formations of the unconscious; rather, the opposite: he was led to it by stumbling against the “therapeutic failure” of the ultimate revelation of sexual meaning. Sexual meanings were revealed, connections leading to it established and reconstructed; yet the problem/symptom persisted.

同样，那种认为“对精神分析来说，（几乎）一切都有性的意义，而理解这种意义就是心理康复的关键”的想法，也犯了同样的错误。要看出它错在哪里，我们只需记住：弗洛伊德是怎样被引向“性本身在构成上就是有问题的”这一理论的。他并不是仅仅靠发现并破译症状和各种无意识形成物“背后”的性的意义，就被引向这个理论；恰恰相反：他是在对性的意义作最终揭示却遭遇“治疗失败”时，撞上了这个理论。性的意义被揭示出来，导向它的联系被建立、被重构；但问题/症状仍然持续。

And this opens the space for a different hypothesis: it is as if sexual meaning, so generously produced by the unconscious, were here to mask the reality of a more fundamental negativity at work in sexuality, to separate us from it by a screen that derives its efficacy from the fact that it is itself a means of satisfaction—satisfaction through meaning, satisfaction in the production of sexual meaning, and (as the obverse of this) in the production of meaning of the sexual. Paradoxical as this might sound, one of the primary tasks of psychoanalysis is to slowly but thoroughly deactivate the path of this satisfaction, to render it useless. To produce sex as absolutely and intrinsically meaningless, not as the ultimate horizon of all humanly produced meaning. That is to say: to restore sex in its dimension of the Real.

这就为另一个假设打开了空间：仿佛性意义——由无意识如此慷慨地生产出来的性意义——在这里就是为了掩盖性中运作着的一种更根本的否定性的现实，用一个屏障把我们与它隔开；而这个屏障之所以有效，恰恰因为它本身就是一种满足的方式——通过意义获得满足，在性意义的生产中获得满足，并且（作为其反面）在对性的意义的生产中获得满足。听起来可能很悖论：精神分析的首要任务之一，就是缓慢而彻底地停用这条满足之路，让它失效。要让性被生产为绝对地、内在地无意义的东西，而不是把它当作人类所生产的一切意义的终极视域。也就是说：把性恢复到它在实在界中的维度。

note：其实也就是批评一下两种典型对“性”的理解，一种是贬低为朴素的实践，一种是笼统为一切意义的原因，实则性是一种不可能——也在这个维度上，性问题就是存在问题。

However, if we accept Zamanian’s thesis that at stake in contemporary psychotherapeutic practices is a “defense” against something involved in the Freudian theory of sexuality, what exactly is this something? For one thing is sure: we must resist the temptation to take the defense against sexuality as self-explanatory; it is not “sex” that can explain that defense; rather, it is the defense that could shed some light on something inherently problematic about the nature of sexuality—something which repeatedly, and as if inevitably, puts us on the track of deeply metaphysical issues.

不过，如果我们接受 Zamanian 的论点：当代心理治疗实践的关键，是对弗洛伊德性欲理论中涉及的某种东西的“防御”，那么这个某种东西究竟是什么？有一点确定无疑：我们必须抵制一种诱惑，即把对性欲的防御看成不言自明；能够解释这一防御的并不是“性”；相反，恰恰是防御才可能让我们看清性欲本性中某个本身就有问题的地方——这个东西一再地、仿佛不可避免地，把我们引向深层的形而上学问题。

note：就和对象a一样，实在界的东西是只能从象征界的裂隙回溯性推断出来的。

Where Do Adults Come From?

One explanation—let us call it the “progressive psychoanalytic explanation”—traces the discomfort in sexuality not so much to the difference as to the irreducible proximity or continuity between infantile and adult sexuality.

成年人从何而来？

一种解释——我们姑且称之为“渐进式精神分析解释”——并不把性欲中的不适主要归因于婴儿性欲与成人性欲之间的差异，而是归因于二者之间不可化约的邻近性或连续性。

The paradoxical status of infantile sexuality as discovered by Freud could be summed up in two points. First, it exists, children are sexual beings; yet secondly, it exists in the absence of both biological and symbolic frameworks for its existence. It exists in the absence of both natural and cultural parameters. Biologically speaking, sexual organs are not up to their function; and symbolically speaking, children have no means of understanding properly and making sense of what is happening to them (sexually). One can understand that this kind of undefined, free-floating zone, unattached to any symbolic chain, can function as particularly sensitive—both in itself as well as in the imagination of adults. But there is a further and more important reason. If infantile sexuality constitutes such a dangerous and sensitive “zone,” it is not simply because of its difference and contrast with adult sexuality but, rather, the opposite: because of their proximity. If infantile sexuality is something that is covered neither by biology nor by the symbolic (“culture”), the next and perhaps greatest scandal of Freudian theory consists in suggesting that, all in all, this state of things doesn’t change all that much when we become adults. The “maturity” of sexual organs dramatically fails to make these organs function as exclusive sites of sexuality, as well as to produce a solid basis for clearly understanding and making sense of our sexuality.

弗洛伊德发现的幼儿性欲，其悖论式地位可以概括为两点。第一，它确实存在，孩子就是有性欲的存在者；第二，它的存在又缺少生物性与象征性两方面的框架来支撑。它是在缺少自然与文化参数的情况下存在的。从生物学上说，性器官还没有达到其功能；从象征上说，孩子也没有办法恰当地理解并赋予发生在自己身上的（性）事情以意义。可以理解，这样一个未被定义、自由漂浮、不附着于任何象征链的区域，会格外敏感——对它自身如此，在成人的想象中也是如此。但还有进一步、也更重要的原因。如果幼儿性欲构成这样一个危险而敏感的区域，那并不只是因为它与成人性欲不同、形成对照，恰恰相反，是因为二者离得太近。如果幼儿性欲既不被生物学也不被象征界（“文化”）所覆盖，那么弗洛伊德理论下一个、也许最大的骇人之处就在于：它暗示，归根到底，当我们成为成人时，这种状况并没有改变多少。性器官的“成熟”明显做不到两件事：让这些器官成为性欲的专属位置，以及为清楚理解和赋予我们的性欲以意义提供一个稳固基础。

Jean Laplanche probably went the furthest to expose this conflict and duality of the sexual by introducing the difference between drive sexuality (which he calls le sexual) and instinctual sexuality (le sexuel). In brief: le sexual is essentially related to different partial drives and their satisfaction; it is not innate, not object-based, and not procreative. It refers to autoerotic, polymorphous, perverse, non-gender-constricted, protean sexuality. Instinctual sexuality, on the other hand, is hormonally based, and more or less preprogrammed. This is the type of sexuality that arrives after prepuberty, that is, after drive or infantile sexuality. So that

When it comes to sexuality, man is subject to the greatest of paradoxes: What is acquired through the drives precedes what is innate and instinctual, in such a way that, at the time it emerges, instinctual sexuality, which is adaptive, finds the seat already taken, as it were, by infantile drives, already and always present in the unconscious. (Laplanche 2002, 49)

让·拉普朗什可能走得最远。他引入“冲动性欲”（他称为 le sexual）与“本能性欲”（le sexuel）的区分，从而揭示了性的这种冲突与二元性。简言之：le sexual 本质上与不同的部分冲动及其满足有关；它不是天生的，不建立在对象上，也不以生殖为目的。它指的是自体情欲的、多形态的、倒错的、不受性别限定的、变化多端的性。另一方面，本能性欲以激素为基础，或多或少是预先编程好的。这类性欲在青春期前阶段之后才出现，也就是在冲动或婴儿性欲之后才出现。因此：

“就性而言，人受制于最大的悖论：通过冲动获得的东西，先于天生和本能的东西；以至于在本能性欲出现时，这种具有适应性的性欲发现，位置可以说已经被婴儿冲动占了；这些冲动已经且始终存在于无意识中。”（Laplanche 2002, 49）

In the same line of reasoning, and based on Freud’s “Three Essays on the Theory of Sexuality,” one can sum these issues up by the following narrative: so-called “genital sexual organization” is far from being primordial. It involves a unification of the originally heterogeneous, dispersed, always-already compound sexual drive, composed of different partial drives, such as looking, touching, sucking, and so on. This unification has two major characteristics: First, it is always a somehow forced and artificial unification (it cannot be viewed simply as a natural teleological result of reproductive maturation). Secondly, it is never really fully achieved or accomplished, which is to say that it never transforms the sexual drive into an organic unity, with all its components ultimately serving one and the same purpose. “Normal,” “healthy” human sexuality is thus a paradoxical, artificial naturalization of the originally denatured drives (denatured in the sense of departing from the “natural” aims of self-preservation and/or the logic of a pure need as unaffected by another supplementary satisfaction). One could even say that human sexuality is “sexual” (and not simply “reproductive”) precisely insofar as the unification at stake, the tying of all the drives to one single purpose, never really works, but allows for different partial drives to continue their circular, self-perpetuating activity.

沿着同样的思路，并根据弗洛伊德的《性学三论》，可以把这些问题概括为下面这种叙述：所谓“生殖器性组织”远不是原初的。它涉及对原初异质、分散、总是已经复合的性冲动的统一；这种性冲动由不同的部分冲动组成，比如看、触摸、吸吮等等。这种统一有两个主要特征：第一，它总是一种多少带有强制性的、人为的统一（不能简单把它看成生殖成熟带来的自然目的论结果）。第二，它从未真正完全实现或完成；也就是说，它从未把性冲动转变为一个有机统一体，让所有组成部分最终都服务于同一个目的。因此，“正常”“健康”的人类性欲，是一种悖论性的、人为的自然化：它把原初已经被去自然化的冲动加以自然化（“去自然化”是指偏离自我保存的“自然”目的，和/或偏离纯粹需要的逻辑，而纯粹需要是不受另一种补充性满足影响的）。甚至可以说，人类性欲之所以是“性的”（而不只是“生殖的”），恰恰是因为这里涉及的那种统一——把所有冲动绑到单一目的上——从未真正成功，反而让不同的部分冲动得以继续它们循环的、自我延续的活动。

Yet there might be something slightly wrong with this narrative, at two points. First, in how it advances from the supposedly original, free, and chaotic multiplicity of the drives to an (always) forced sexual unification; and, secondly, in the way in which (much in line with Laplanche and his notion of “le sexual”) it situates the properly (or “humanly”) sexual of sexuality simply on the side of the drives and their satisfaction (as opposed to instinctual/reproductive sexuality). It is not that this is simply wrong; rather, things are slightly more complicated, and something crucial is missing in this account. This something concerns precisely the point of the encounter between the enjoyment involved in the drives (the surplus pleasure or the “other satisfaction” that tends to be produced in the process of the satisfaction of vital needs) and sexuality. It is precisely at this point that the strongest resistance to the notion of infantile sexuality is at work: What is it that makes, for example, the child’s sucking of its thumb (or any other pleasure-seeking activity) sexual? Is it simply that we can deduce this retroactively from the adult sexuality in which these surplus satisfactions carried by the drives play an obvious and important role? This seems to be the answer of what I referred to above as the “progressive psychoanalytic explanation”: if we look at adult sexuality, we can see that many of its elements (that is, many ways of finding satisfaction) are things that children “practice” as well, which clearly indicates the existence of some kind of continuity.

不过，这个叙述可能有两个地方不太对。第一，它如何从被设想为原初、自由而混乱的冲动多重性，推进到一种（总是）被迫的性统一化；第二，它（与 拉普朗什 及其“le sexual”概念很一致）把性本身中真正（或“属人”）的性，简单放在冲动及其满足这一边（与本能/生殖的性相对）。这并不是说它完全错；而是事情稍微更复杂，这个说法漏掉了某个关键的东西。这个关键的东西，恰恰涉及冲动中的享乐（在生命需要得到满足的过程中往往会产生的剩余快感，或“另一种满足”）与性之间的相遇点。正是在这一点上，对婴儿性欲概念最强的抵抗在起作用：例如，究竟是什么让孩子吮拇指（或任何别的寻求快感的活动）成为性的？是不是仅仅因为我们可以从成人性欲中回溯地推出这一点，而在成人性欲里，冲动所携带的这些剩余满足扮演着明显而重要的角色？这似乎就是我上面所说的“渐进式精神分析解释”的答案：如果我们看成人性欲，就能看到它的许多要素（也就是许多找到满足的方式）也是孩子会“做”的，这清楚表明存在某种连续性。

One major drawback of this linear account of sexuality and its development is that it leaves out completely the central concept of psychoanalysis, that is, the unconscious. Repression, it would seem, can enter this account only as repression performed on the sexual (content or activity), not as intrinsically and constitutively bound up with it. Hence the value of Laplanche’s adjustment of this theory, which could be briefly put as follows: (Infantile) enjoyment is sexual because it is contaminated, from the very outset, by way of the child’s universe being constantly intruded upon by “enigmatic signifiers,” that is, by the unconscious and sexually charged messages of adults.3 In other words, it is not pleasure or satisfaction as such, but the unconscious that makes a pleasure “sexual.” The further crucial point is that these “messages” are not enigmatic only for children, but for the adults emitting them as well—this is perhaps the most fundamental example of the famous Hegelian dictum that the secrets of the Egyptians were secrets for the Egyptians themselves. What sexualizes the pleasure experienced by children is thus first and foremost the encounter with the unconscious of adults; not an encounter with an additional (“adult”) surplus knowledge (incomprehensible to children and hence “enigmatic”), but with a minus, with something that first comes to them only as missing from its place in the Other. Infantile activities that seek pleasure for the sake of pleasure are “sexual” because of their entanglement with the signifiers which, by default, involve and support the unconscious of the Other. To repeat: what makes the enjoyment related to the drives sexual is its relation to the unconscious (in its very ontological negativity) and not, for example, its entanglement and contamination with sexuality in the narrower sense of the term (relating to sexual organs and sexual intercourse).

这种关于性欲及其发展的线性叙述有一个重大缺陷：它完全漏掉了精神分析的核心概念，也就是无意识。在这个叙述里，压抑似乎只能作为对性（性内容或性活动）的压抑出现，而不能作为与性内在地、构成性地绑在一起的东西出现。所以，拉普朗什 对这一理论的调整就有价值，可以简单说成：（婴儿期）享乐是性的，因为它从一开始就被污染了：孩子的世界不断被“谜样能指”侵入，也就是被成人无意识的、带性负荷的信息侵入。换言之，使一种快乐成为“性的”，不是快乐或满足本身，而是无意识。更进一步的关键点是：这些“信息”不只对孩子是谜样的，对发出它们的成人也一样谜样——这或许是黑格尔那句名言最根本的例子：埃及人的秘密对埃及人自己也是秘密。因此，性化儿童所体验的快乐的，首先是遭遇成人的无意识；不是遭遇一种额外的（“成人的”）剩余知识（孩子无法理解，所以显得“谜样”），而是遭遇一个减号，遭遇某种最初只能以“在大他者中缺席”的方式来到他们那里的东西。婴儿那些为快乐而快乐的活动之所以是“性的”，是因为它们与能指纠缠在一起；这些能指默认就涉及并支撑大他者的无意识。再重复一遍：使与冲动相关的享乐成为性的，是它与无意识的关系（就在其本体论否定性中），而不是例如它与狭义性欲——与性器官和性交有关的性欲——的纠缠和污染。

The unconscious thus enters our horizon as the unconscious of the Other; it does not start with the first thing we repress, it starts (for us) with repression as the signifying form pertaining to discursivity as such. (Further on in our argument we will relate this to the concept of a “minus one,” which plays a crucial role in Lacan’s later work.) The unconscious comes to us from the outside. This also constitutes a strong (Lacanian) reading of the Freudian (hypo)thesis of the Urverdrängung, primal repression as the ground and condition of all repression proper. In his conceptualizations of the unconscious and of repression, Freud was led to introduce the hypothesis according to which what we usually refer to as repression is actually and already an “after-pressure” (Nachdrängen). Actual repression or repression proper is already based on repression; repression is constitutively redoubled (Freud 2001a, 148, 180). Moreover, Freud emphasizes that it is a mistake to focus the discussion of repression solely on “the repulsion which operates from the direction of conscious upon what is to be repressed; quite as important is the attraction exercised by what is primally repressed upon everything with which it can establish a connection” (ibid., 148). Already in Seminar XI Lacan will radicalize this Freudian hypothesis by linking it directly to the signifying structure and to “a necessary fall of the first signifier” (Lacan 1987, 251). This “fall” coincides with the constitution of the subject: “the subject is constituted around the Urverdrängung.” In other words, Urverdrängung is not a repression “performed” by the subject, but coincides with its emergence. And already here Lacan briefly introduces a theme that will become much more prominent in his later seminars, relating this instance to the Kantian notion of “negative quantities,” pointing out that “minus one (−1) is not zero” (ibid., 252).

因此，无意识是作为大他者的无意识进入我们视野的；它并非从我们压抑的第一样东西开始，而是（对我们而言）始于压抑，而压抑是属于话语性本身的能指形式。（在后文中，我们会把这与“负一”的概念联系起来；“负一”在拉康后期工作中起着关键作用。）无意识从外部来到我们这里。这也构成对弗洛伊德关于原初压抑（Urverdrängung）的（假）设的一种强的（拉康式）解读：原初压抑是一切严格意义上的压抑的基础与条件。在弗洛伊德对无意识和压抑的概念化中，他被引导去引入这样一个假设：我们通常所说的压抑，实际上已经是一种“后压”（Nachdrängen）。实际的压抑，或严格意义上的压抑，已经建立在压抑之上；压抑在构成上被加倍了（Freud 2001a, 148, 180）。此外，弗洛伊德强调，把压抑的讨论只集中在“从意识方向对将要被压抑之物运作的排斥”上是错误的；同样重要的是“原初被压抑之物对一切能与它建立联系的东西所施加的吸引”（ibid., 148）。在《研讨班XI》中，拉康已经把这个弗洛伊德式假设激进化：他把它直接与能指结构、以及“第一个能指的必然坠落”联系起来（Lacan 1987, 251）。这个“坠落”与主体的构成同时发生：“主体是围绕原初压抑被构成的。”换言之，原初压抑不是主体“施行”的压抑，而是与主体的出现同时发生。也正是在这里，拉康简要引入了一个后来在其研讨班中会变得更突出的主题：他把这一环节与康德的“负量”概念联系起来，并指出“负一（−1）不是零”（ibid., 252）。

And it is in this perspective that we should understand one of the key emphases of this book: that something concerning sexuality is constitutively unconscious. That is to say: unconscious even when it first occurs, and not simply due to a subsequent repression. There is something about sexuality that appears only as repressed, something that registers in reality only in the form of repression (and not as something that first is, and is then repressed). And it is this something (and not some positive feature) that makes sexuality “sexual” in the strong meaning of the word. This is to say that the relation between the unconscious and sexuality is not that between some content and its container; sexuality pertains to the very being-there of the unconscious, in its very ontological uncertainty.

正是在这个视角下，我们才应理解本书的一个关键重点：与性有关的某个东西，在构成上就是无意识的。也就是说：即便它初次发生时，它就已经是无意识的，而不只是由于后来才发生的压抑。关于性的某个东西只以被压抑的形式出现，它在现实中只能以压抑的形式被登记（而不是先作为某个东西存在，然后才被压抑）。正是这个“某物”（而不是某种实证性的特征）使性在强意义上成为“性”。也就是说，无意识与性之间的关系，并不是内容与容器之间的关系；性关涉的是无意识本身的“在那里存在”，关涉其本体论上的不确定性本身。

note：阳具能指Φ/对象a。

Before we examine this in more detail, we will briefly stop to consider another problematic notion related to sexuality: the much too simple opposition between the “subversive” chaotic multiplicity of the drives and “normative sexuality.” It is beyond question that a lot of violence has always been done in the name of the alleged norm, but the question remains as to what is it that binds this norm to such violence: is it simply fear of the “untamed” multiplicity of the drives, or is it something else?

在进一步细看之前，我们先停一下，看看与性有关的另一个有问题的说法：把冲动的“颠覆性”混沌多重性与“规范性性欲”对立起来——这种对立实在太过简单。毫无疑问，一直以来，大量暴力都打着所谓规范的旗号；但问题仍然是：究竟是什么把这种规范与这样的暴力绑在一起？它仅仅是对冲动那种“未被驯服”的多重性的恐惧，还是别的东西？

Christianity and Polymorphous Perversity

基督教与多形性倒错

According to the common perception, cultural (social, moral, religious) normativity promotes so-called natural sexuality (heterosexual intercourse) and tends to ban or repress drive sexuality, which is seen as perverse, asocial, serving no purpose outside itself, and hence escaping individual and social means of control. … But is this really so? Is it not possible that—beyond a very superficial level—this perception could be dramatically wrong? Christianity is usually taken as the magisterial example of the kind of attitude that bans drive sexuality and promotes only “purposeful” reproductive coupling. Yet it suffices to shift the perspective just a little bit (and at the right end), as Lacan does in the following passage, to get a very different picture:

Christ, even when resurrected from the dead, is valued for his body, and his body is the means by which communion in his presence is incorporation—oral drive—with which Christ’s wife, the Church as it is called, contents itself very well, having nothing to expect from copulation.
In everything that followed from the effects of Christianity, particularly in πart … everything is exhibition of the body evoking jouissance—and you can lend credence to the testimony of someone who has just come back from an orgy of churches in Italy—but without copulation. If copulation isn’t present, it’s no accident. It’s just as much out of place there as it is in human reality, to which it nevertheless provides sustenance with the fantasies by which that reality is constituted. (Lacan 1999, 113)

按通常看法，文化（社会、道德、宗教）的规范性会提倡所谓的自然性欲（异性性交），并倾向于禁止或压抑冲动性性欲；后者被视为倒错的、非社会的、在自身之外没有目的，因而逃逸于个体和社会的控制手段之外。……但真是这样吗？有没有可能——只要越过很表层的层面——这种看法会错得离谱？基督教通常被当作权威范例，代表那种禁止冲动性性欲、只推崇“有目的的”生殖配对的态度。然而，只要稍微变换一下视角（而且换到正确的一端），像拉康在下面一段所做的那样，就能得到一幅大不相同的图景：

基督即便从死里复活，被看重的仍是他的身体；而他的身体就是手段：在他的临在中的共融由此成为摄入——即口欲冲动。基督的妻子，也就是所谓的教会，对这种摄入心满意足，完全不指望性交。
在基督教效果所带来的一切之中，尤其是在艺术中……一切都成了身体的展示，唤起享乐——你完全可以相信一个刚从意大利教堂狂欢中回来的人的证词——但没有性交。如果性交不在场，这并非偶然。性交在那里和在人的现实中一样不合时宜；不过，正是通过构成人的现实的那些幻想，性交还是给人的现实提供了养料。（Lacan 1999, 113）

note：在场的肉体的影响。

What is the point of this amazing passage, which rings so true? On the one hand, there is nothing necessarily asocial in partial drives: as autofocused as they may well be, they can nevertheless function as the glue of society, as the very stuff of communion. On the other hand, there seems to be something profoundly disruptive at stake in “copulation.” For the kind of (social) bond it proposes, Christianity does not need copulation, which functions as the superfluous element, something on top of what would be (ideally) needed, and hence as disturbing. This is why even the “purest” sort of procreative sexual copulation is connected with sin. Or, as Saint Augustine has famously pointed out:Sexuality is not the original sin (the latter refers to the original pair’s disobedience when eating from the tree of knowledge), but the punishment for it, and the locus of its perpetuation—it is a subsequent addition to the original creation. In other words, in Saint Augustine’s account, sexuality itself is problematic enough to be seen as a punishment, a curse.

这段精彩的文字为何如此真切，它的要点究竟在哪里？一方面，部分冲动并不必然是非社会的：它们尽可以高度自我聚焦，却仍能充当社会的黏合剂，甚至成为共融的质料。另一方面，“交媾”中似乎总有某种极具扰乱性的东西在起作用。就基督教所提出的那种（社会）纽带而言，它并不需要交媾；交媾是多余元素，是加在（理想上）本已所需之物之上的东西，因此令人不安。正因如此，哪怕是最“纯粹”的、以生育为目的的交媾，也与罪相关。或者，正如圣奥古斯丁著名地指出：性不是原罪（后者指的是原初那一对在吃知识树果实时的不服从），而是对原罪的惩罚，也是原罪得以延续的场所——它是对原初创造的后续添加。换言之，在圣奥古斯丁的叙述中，性本身已经足够成问题，足以被看作一种惩罚、一种诅咒。

Indeed, favored as it is in religion’s doxa, “natural (procreative) intercourse” is utterly banned from the religious imaginary, whereas this same imaginary does not recede from, for example, images of canonized saints eating the excrements of another person5—an action that is usually cataloged at the very peak of perversions. If we take a look at famous stories (and pictures) of Christian martyrdom, they are surprisingly full of partial objects in the strict Freudian meaning of the term: a real treasury of images of objects related to different partial drives. Saint Agatha’s cut-off breast and Saint Lucy’s gouged-out eyes are two of the most well-known images, portrayed hundreds of times by different artists.6 Here are just two examples.

确实，尽管“自然（生殖性）交合”在宗教教义中备受推崇，它却完全被排除在宗教想象界之外；而同一个想象界却不回避——比如——被封圣的圣人吃他人排泄物的图像，这种行为通常被归到倒错的最顶端。如果我们看看著名的基督教殉道故事（以及图画），会惊讶地发现它们充满严格弗洛伊德意义上的部分对象：真是一座与不同部分冲动相关的对象图像的宝库。圣阿加莎被割下的乳房、圣露西被挖出的眼睛，是其中最广为人知的两个图像，被不同艺术家描绘过数百次。这里只举两个例子。

Viewed from this perspective, Christianity can indeed appear to be centered around the “jouissance of the body.” Partial drives and the passion or satisfaction they procure are abundantly present in many aspects of Christianity, and constitute an important part of its official imaginary. In this precise sense, one could even go so far as to say that in its libidinal aspect the Christian religion massively relies on what belongs to the register of “infantile sexuality” (defined by Freud as polymorphous perversity), that is, to the satisfaction and bonds derived from partial objects, with the exclusion of sexual coupling. Pure enjoyment, “enjoyment for the sake of enjoyment,” is not exactly what is banned here; what is banned, or repressed, is its link to sexuality.

从这个视角看，基督教确实可以显得是围绕“身体的享乐”组织起来的。部分冲动，以及它们带来的激情或满足，在基督教的许多方面都大量存在，并构成其官方想象界的重要部分。正是在这个确切意义上，人们甚至可以进一步说：在其力比多方面，基督教宗教大量依赖属于“婴儿性欲”界域的东西（弗洛伊德把它界定为多形倒错），也就是说，依赖由部分对象得来的满足与联结，同时排除性结合。这里被禁止的，并不完全是纯粹享乐，即“为享乐而享乐”；被禁止或被压抑的，是它跟性的联系。

note：基督教没有禁止享乐，而是把享乐从性交中剥离出来，允许人们通过一些行动去体验类似婴儿期的、局部的、升华了的肉体与心理满足。

In other words, it is clearly of paramount importance for the Christian religion not to acknowledge these polymorphously perverse satisfactions of the drives as sexual, while not banning them in themselves. But why exactly? Why this necessity not simply to fight all enjoyment, as is often erroneously believed, but to separate enjoyment from sexuality as neatly as possible; that is, to refuse to conceive of it in sexual terms? It is as if the strong social pressure put on “natural sexuality” (copulation) to function as the norm were there to hide an abyssal negativity of natural sexuality itself, much more than to keep the supposedly disruptive partial drives away.

换句话说，对基督教而言，显然极其重要的是：不把冲动的这些多形倒错满足认作是性的，同时又不禁止这些满足本身。但究竟为什么？为什么有必要不是简单地对抗一切享乐——人们常常误以为如此——而是尽可能干净地把享乐与性欲分开；也就是说，拒绝用性的框架来设想享乐？这就像是：社会对“自然性欲”（交配）施加强大压力，要它充当规范，与其说是为了把那些被设想为扰乱性的部分冲动挡在外面，不如说是为了遮蔽自然性欲本身的深渊式否定性。

In other words, this questioning brings us not so much to the cultural as to the “natural” aspect of sexuality. It is as if this “natural” aspect were in fact the most problematic, the most uncertain. There seems to be something in nature itself that is dramatically wrong at this point. The problem is not simply that nature is “always-already cultural,” but rather that nature lacks something in order to be Nature (our Other) in the first place. Culture is not something that mediates, splits, denatures natural sexuality (as supposedly present in animals, for instance); it is being generated at the very locus where something in nature (as sexual nature) is lacking.

换句话说，这一追问与其说把我们带向性的文化面，不如说带向性的“自然”面。仿佛这个“自然”面其实才是最麻烦、最不确定的。自然本身在这一点上好像出了严重问题。问题不只是自然“总是已经文化的”，而是：自然为了首先成为自然（我们的大他者），本身就缺了某物。文化并不是对自然性欲进行中介、切割、去自然化的东西（比如人们以为动物身上现成就有那种自然性欲）；文化恰恰是在自然（作为性的自然）中某物缺失的那个位置上被生成出来的。

note：当下的文化为了掩盖性的不可能，往往的做法就是淫荡化性，遮蔽它。

One way of putting this would be to say that there is no sexual instinct, that is, no knowledge (“law”) inherent to sexuality which would be able to reliably guide it. Yet this claim can itself be understood in two ways. According to the usual perspective, this lack of sexual instinct (as a reliable autopilot) is perceived as something specifically human, induced by the human constitution (and the culture following from it). In this line of reasoning we usually say that while there is sexual instinct in Nature (in animals), there is none in human beings (who are thus the point of exception in respect to Nature). Humanity, at its most fundamental level, is thus seen as a deviation from Nature, and notably from the Animal. With humanity, something particular occurs that makes it decline from Nature, and complicates the way the laws of Nature function on the territory of the human. We will discuss this human/animal difference in more detail in chapter 4, so let us just briefly sketch out here what could be another possible perspective on this: to conceive humanity not as an exception to Nature, but as that point of Nature where its lack of “knowledge” (of sexual law) acquires a singular epistemic form. In this perspective, humanity is not an exception to Nature, a deviation from it, but the point of a specific articulation of Nature’s own inherent negativity. There is knowledge in Nature (“knowledge in the real,” as Lacan calls it), but this knowledge is lacking at the point of sexuation, and that includes sexuated animals.

换一种说法就是：不存在性本能，也就是说，不存在内在于性之中的、能够可靠地引导性的知识（“法则”）。不过，这个说法本身可以有两种理解。按通常的看法，性本能的缺失（即缺少一个可靠的“自动驾驶”）被看成人类特有的东西，由人的构造（以及随之而来的文化）造成。沿着这条思路，我们通常会说：自然中有性本能（动物有），而人没有（于是人成了相对于自然的例外点）。因此，在最根本的层面上，人被看作对自然的偏离，尤其是对动物的偏离。在人这里发生了某种特别的事情，使人从自然中坠落，并使自然法则在人的领域中的运作方式变得复杂。我们会在第四章更详细地讨论人与动物的差异，所以这里只简要勾画另一种可能的看法：不把人类设想为自然的例外，而把它设想为自然的一个点——在这一点上，自然对“知识”（关于性法则的知识）的缺乏获得了一种独特的认识形式。在这个视角下，人类不是自然的例外、不是对自然的偏离，而是自然自身固有否定性得以特定接合的那个点。自然中有知识（拉康称之为“实在界中的知识”），但这种知识在性化的点上缺失，而这包括已经性化的动物。

note：我猜测是人类对性的态度反而彰显了性的不可能，回溯性推断。

What, then, would be the difference between the human animal and other (sexual) animals? A difference based not on human exception from nature, but on a different kind of articulation of a certain impasse of sexuated nature as such? Our answer will go in the following direction: human sexuality is the point at which the impossibility (ontological negativity) pertaining to the sexual relation appears as such, “registers” in reality as its part. It registers in the singular form discovered by Freud as that of the unconscious.

那么，人类动物与其他（有性）动物之间的差别究竟会是什么？这种差别是否并非基于人类例外于自然，而是基于对性化自然本身的某种僵局的不同接合方式？我们的回答将朝以下方向展开：人类性欲正是这样一个点——与性关系相关的不可能性（本体论否定性）作为其本身在此显现，并作为现实的一部分“登记”在现实中。它以一种由弗洛伊德发现的独特形式被登记下来，即无意识的形式。

If we start out from a fundamental lack of knowledge (instinct) in nature at the point of sexuation (nature doesn’t know how to be in the sexual sense, and we share this with other sexed animals), the difference is the difference between two ways of not knowing: a simple way, and a way that actually involves a singular kind of knowledge, namely, the unconscious. Animals do not know (that they do not know). Not completely in jest, we could say that sexuality is not problematic for animals because they do not know that it actually is problematic.What distinguishes the human animal is that it knows (that it doesn’t know). Yet at stake here is not simply that humans are aware, conscious of this lack of sexual knowledge in nature; rather, the right way of putting it would be to say that they are “unconscious of it” (which is not the same as saying that we are not conscious of it). The unconscious (in its very form) is the “positive” way in which the ontological negativity of a given reality registers in this reality itself, and it registers in a way which does not rely on the simple opposition between knowing and not knowing, between being or not being aware of something. And the reason is that what is at stake is precisely not “something” (some thing, some fact that we could be aware of or not) but a negativity that is itself perceptible only through its own negation. To be “unconscious of something” does not mean simply that one does not know it; rather, it implies a paradoxical redoublement, and is itself twofold or split: it involves not knowing that we know (… that we don’t know). This is one of the best definitions of the unconscious (Žižek 2008, 457). As Lacan put it, unconscious knowledge is a knowledge that does not know itself.

如果我们从这一点出发：在性化点上，自然中存在一种根本的知识（本能）缺失——自然在性的意义上不知道如何存在，而我们与其他有性动物共享这一点——那么区别就在于两种不知的方式之间的差异：一种是简单的不知，另一种则实际涉及一种独特的知识，即无意识。动物不知道（自己不知道）。我们可以说——不完全是开玩笑——性对动物并不构成问题，因为它们不知道它其实是个问题。人类动物之所以不同，在于它知道（自己不知道）。但这里关键并不只是：人类觉察到、意识到自然中这种性的知识缺失；更准确的说法是，他们“对其无意识”（这不等于说我们没有意识到它）。无意识（就其形式本身而言）是某一既定现实的本体论否定性在这现实自身中被铭写的“肯定”方式；而且它的铭写方式并不依赖知与不知、对某物有意识或无意识之间的简单对立。原因在于，这里的关键恰恰不是“某物”（某个东西、某个我们可以意识到或意识不到的事实），而是一种只有通过自身否定才可感知的否定性。对某物“无意识”并不只是说一个人不知道它；它意味着一种悖论性的双重化，本身是双重的、分裂的：它涉及“不知道我们知道”（……“不知道我们不知道”）。这是无意识最好的定义之一（Žižek 2008, 457）。如拉康所说，无意识知识是一种不知道自身的知识。

The singular and revolutionary Freudian notion of the unconscious is thus not simply about not knowing as opposed to knowing. It is about a singular form of not-knowing as a form of knowing. There is a particular kind of knowledge that exists only in, and as, the very form of the unconscious, its work and its formations. And I am not talking about some kind of prereflective intuition—the latter may well exist, but it has nothing to do with the unconscious and its structure. The unconscious is the very form of existence of an ontological negativity pertaining to sexuality (“there is no sexual relation”). Because of its link to a singular mode/split of knowledge (I don’t know that I know), this form is actually epistemic.

因此，弗洛伊德式无意识概念那种独特而革命性的地方，并不只是说它讲“不知”与“知”相对立。它讲的是一种独特的“不知”形式，本身就作为一种“知”的形式。有一种特别的知识，只存在于无意识的形式之中，并且只能作为这种形式——作为无意识的运作及其形成物——而存在。我并不是在说某种前反思的直觉——那种东西也许存在，但它跟无意识及其结构没有关系。无意识是那种与性相关的本体论否定性的存在形式本身（“不存在性关系”）。由于它连接着知识的独特模式/分裂（“我不知道自己知道”），这种形式实际上是认识论性的。

Let us now link this to our previous investigation: what can it tell us about the functioning of the norm (of purely reproductive coupling) in the Christian tradition? What exactly is being banned or veiled by this norm? It seems to concern precisely the ontological negativity of sexuation and sexuality as such. What one attempts to hide or repress in imposing the norm (of purely reproductive coupling) is not simply something else (for instance, a perverse debauchery, or pure self-perpetuating enjoyment), but rather the something which is not there (something missing). In other words: what is being banned is not the Signifier of the sexual (or its Image), but rather the (unconscious) knowledge of the nonexistence of such a Signifier. Sexuality is regulated in all kinds of ways not because of its debauchery, but insofar as it implies (and “transmits”) the knowledge of this ontological negativity.

现在让我们把这与先前的考察联系起来：关于基督教传统中（纯粹生殖性结合的）规范如何运作，它能告诉我们什么？这个规范究竟禁止或遮蔽了什么？看起来，它恰恰涉及性化与性本身的本体论否定性。人们在强加这一规范时试图隐藏或压抑的，并不只是别的东西（例如某种倒错的淫荡，或纯粹自我延续的享乐），而是那个并不在场的东西（某种缺失）。换句话说：被禁止的并不是性的能指（或它的意象），而是关于这种能指并不存在的（无意识）知识。性以各种方式被规制，并不是因为它的淫荡，而是因为它蕴含（并“传递”）着这种本体论否定性的知识。

The quintessential biblical story that presents sexuality and knowledge as inseparably bound to the scene of original sin is thus pointing in the right direction. What exactly is it that Adam and Eve got to know when they ate from the tree of knowledge? In the Bible this is not clear at all. What is clear, however, is that the expression “good and evil” (rov wa-ra)—in the formulation “tree of the knowledge of good and evil”—does not refer to the knowing of, and distinguishing between, good and evil (this would already be a highly tendentious reading), but is actually a fixed expression denoting “everything” (as when we say “this and that”). Thus all the Bible tells us is that when they ate from the tree of knowledge, knowledge was imparted to Adam and Eve. And if we venture a Lacanian reading of this, we could add: not knowledge of this or that particular thing, but the (signifying) structure of knowledge as such. And what comes with the “(signifying) structure of knowledge as such” is the gap of the unconscious: the latter is precisely what distinguishes knowledge from information or data. In other words, what was transmitted to them was precisely the gap of the Urverdrängung as constitutive of knowledge. And this is why, once they ate from the tree of knowledge, the immediate result was an affect, namely that they found the sight of their naked bodies shameful. This shame is properly ontological: it appears at the place of the lacking signifier (−1), because of the signifying lack built into the structure of knowledge: it appears because no signifier appears there. …

这个典型的圣经故事把性与知识不可分割地系在原罪场景上，因此它指向了正确的方向。那么，当亚当和夏娃从知识树上吃下果子时，他们究竟知道了什么？在《圣经》里，这一点根本不清楚。但清楚的是，“善与恶”（rov wa-ra）这个说法——在“善恶知识树”这一表述中——并不是指知道并区分善与恶（那已经是一种很有倾向性的读法），而其实是一个固定说法，表示“一切”（就像我们说“这个那个”）。所以，《圣经》告诉我们的全部内容只是：当他们吃了知识树的果子，知识就被传给了亚当和夏娃。如果我们大胆对此作一种拉康式阅读，还可以补充：传给他们的不是关于这个或那个具体事物的知识，而是知识本身的（能指）结构。而伴随“知识本身的（能指）结构”而来的，就是无意识的缝隙：正是后者把知识与信息或数据区分开来。换句话说，传给他们的正是作为知识之构成的原初压抑（Urverdrängung）的缝隙。这就是为什么，他们一旦吃了知识树的果子，直接结果就是一种情感：他们觉得看到自己赤裸的身体很羞耻。这种羞耻恰切地说是本体论的：它出现在缺失的能指（−1）的位置上，因为知识结构中内建着能指缺失；它出现，是因为那里没有能指出现……

This significant link between knowledge and sex is not limited in the Bible only to the scene of original sin, but has a further and repetitive insistence. Hence the idiom “knowledge in the biblical sense.” This particular way in which the Bible refers to sexual intercourse (as “knowing the other”) is obviously not of the same kind as the other most common euphemisms for intercourse that we find there: “to enter,” “to lie with,” and “to go into.” These are simply descriptive euphemisms. On the other hand, could we not recognize, in the way knowledge is used to refer to sexual intercourse, a signifying trace of the sexual relation falling into its own ontological void, which registers only as a peculiar (negative) epistemological score? That is to say: “knowing the other in the biblical sense” is to engage with the point in the Other where knowledge is lacking. And from the religious perspective, this lack of knowledge in the Other (missing signifier of the sexual relation) is no small matter. Hence the shame. The sight of naked bodies is not “shameful” because of these bodies as such, but because of what these naked bodies fail to convey, namely, the sexual relation.

知识与性之间的这一重要联系，在《圣经》里并不只限于原罪场景，而是还进一步、反复地坚持自身。因此才有“《圣经》意义上的认识”这一说法。《圣经》用这种方式来指性交（即“认识他者”），显然不同于我们在其中见到的其他最常见的性交委婉语：“进入”、“同寝”、“进去”。这些只是描述性的委婉说法。另一方面，我们难道不能在“用知识来指性交”的方式里，辨认出性关系的一道能指痕迹吗？——性关系坠入自身的本体论虚空，只被登记为一种奇特的、（否定性的）认识论记录。也就是说，“在《圣经》意义上认识他者”，就是去触及大他者中知识缺失的那个点。而从宗教视角看，大他者中这种知识缺失（性关系之能指的缺失）绝不是小事。羞耻由此而来。看到赤裸的身体之所以“可耻”，并不是因为这些身体本身，而是因为这些赤裸身体没能传达出来的东西，即性关系。

We find a similar constellation if we consider sexuality on the level of the imaginary: all we can see in representations of sexuality is bodies enjoying parts of other bodies. In Lacan’s words:

As is emphasized admirably by the kind of Kantian that Sade was, one can only enjoy a part of the Other’s body, for the simple reason that one has never seen a body completely wrap itself around the Other’s body, to the point of surrounding and phagocytizing it. That is why we must confine ourselves to simply giving it a little squeeze, like that, taking a forearm or anything else—ouch! (Lacan 1999, 23)

如果在想象界层面来考察性，我们会发现一种相似的构型：在性的各种表象里，我们能看到的只是身体在享乐其他身体的某些部分。用拉康的话说：

正如萨德这位多少算得上康德主义者的人精彩地强调的那样，人只能享乐大他者身体的一部分，原因很简单：从来没人见过一个身体完全包裹住大他者的身体，乃至把它围住、吞噬掉。所以我们只能止步于简单捏它一下，就这样，抓住一条前臂或别的什么——哎哟！（Lacan 1999, 23）

The norm (normative prescriptions of sexuality) emerges precisely at the point of this lack in the representation. More precisely, the norm could be seen as taking the place of the image that “one has never seen,” that of a body completely wrapping itself around the Other’s body. This image, in its very impossibility, is its other side, it is the fantasmatic underpinning of the norm; it is what helps to sustain the norm, and our complicity in it. It is the fantasy sustained by the very imposition of the norm, and sustaining the norm in turn: the fantasy of the sexual relation.

规范（性欲的规范性规定）恰恰出现在表征中这个缺失的点上。更准确地说，可以把规范看作占据了那个“从未见过”的形象的位置，即一个身体完全包裹住大他者身体的位置。这个形象正因其不可能性，成为规范的另一面；它是规范的幻想性支撑；它帮助维持规范，也维持我们对规范的共谋。这个幻想由规范的强加本身所维持，又反过来维持规范：它就是性关系的幻想。

The further crucial thing to point out at this juncture, however, is that non-relation is not simply an absence of relation, but is itself a real, even the Real. What does this mean? We must not make the mistake of conceiving the existence of the sexual relation as a fantasy, which psychoanalysis would invite us to get rid of, and to accept instead the reality of partial drives and fleeting pleasures (“squeezing” here and there) as the ultimate raw reality, as all there is. The sexual relation is not simply a fantasy of something that is not there, but which we would, for some reason, like to be there (why exactly we would be so eager for it to exist is not clear in this sort of argument). Why would something like the nonexistence of the sexual relation be “unbearable,” if we can get actual satisfaction by “squeezing” here and there, by enjoying parts of the other’s body (or our own, for that matter)? The lack of sexual relation is real in the sense that, as lack or negativity, it is built into what is there, determining its logic and structure in an important way. This brings us back to the erroneous opposition between what seems to be a full positivity of the drives (in their very partial character) and the negativity at work in sexuality (as relational). What psychoanalysis teaches us is not that, because of this non-relation, we have access to only partial and fleeting pleasures and satisfactions (“squeezing” here and there). The claim is stronger: these partial pleasures and satisfactions are already (in-)formed by the negativity implied by the non-relation. They do not exist independently of it, so that we could have recourse to them, for lack of anything better. They are essentially and intrinsically constituted by “the lack of anything better”: they are the way in which the lack of anything better (the lack of sexual substance or signifier) exists in reality. It is not—to put it simply—that we have, on the one hand, the pure positivity of the drives and their satisfactions and, on the other, this (catastrophic) idea that we need something else or more, namely, for them to form or represent a relation. And since this does not happen, we feel bad and erect the fantasy of the relation. The fantasy (and imperative) of the relation comes from (within) the very structuring of the drives. Let us now look more closely at what this could mean.

不过，这里还要指出的另一个关键点是：非关系并不只是关系的不在场，它本身就是一种实在，甚至是实在界。这是什么意思？我们不要犯错，把性关系的存在当成一种幻想，以为精神分析会邀请我们摆脱它，转而接受部分冲动和转瞬即逝的快感（这里捏一下、那里捏一下）的实在性，把它当作最终赤裸的现实、当作全部。性关系并不只是对某个不在之物的幻想——我们不知为何希望它在（在这种论证里，我们为什么如此渴望它存在，其实并不清楚）。如果我们可以通过到处“捏一把”、通过享用他人身体的部分（或我们自己的身体）而获得实际满足，那么像“性关系不存在”这样的事为什么会“难以承受”？性关系的缺失是实在的，意思是：作为缺失或否定性，它被内建在现存之物中，并以重要方式决定后者的逻辑和结构。这把我们带回一个错误对立：一边似乎是冲动的完整肯定性（恰恰在其部分性中），另一边是性（作为关系性的）中运作的否定性。精神分析教给我们的并不是：因为这种非关系，我们只能获得部分而短暂的快感和满足（这里捏一下、那里捏一下）。它的主张更强：这些部分快感和满足已经被非关系所蕴含的否定性（从内部）塑形。它们并不独立于非关系而存在，好像我们因为别无更好选择才退而求其次。它们在本质上、内在地由“没有更好的东西”构成：它们就是“没有更好的东西”（性实体或能指的缺失）在现实中存在的方式。简单说，并不是一边有冲动及其满足的纯粹肯定性，另一边又有这个（灾难性的）观念：我们需要别的东西或更多东西，也就是需要它们形成或代表一种关系；而既然这没有发生，我们就感觉糟糕，并树立起性关系的幻想。性关系的幻想（以及律令）来自冲动结构化本身，从其内部产生。现在让我们更仔细地看看，这可能意味着什么。

note：面对这套解释的错误想法往往是菲勒斯享乐本身作为一种部分享乐，那么就一定存在一种更多的享乐形式——并不是的，即便是大他者享乐也是不存在的。

Chapter 2

… and Even Stranger out There

The Quandary of the Relation

Let us return to John Huston’s movie Freud: The Secret Passion. The remark of the chairman, trying to restore order in the passionate outrage provoked by Freud’s lecture on infantile sexuality (“Gentlemen, we are not in a political meeting!”), does indeed point us in a most interesting direction, that of a surprising coincidence between politics and the (Freudian) theory of sexuality. It is as if every time one reopens the question of sexuality, something is decided that is of a political order. This certainly held true for the politics of the psychoanalytic movement itself, and for the ruptures it produced within the movement. But it might also be true in the more specific sense of politics as referring to what can be articulated around some fundamental social antagonism(s).

让我们回到 John Huston 的电影《弗洛伊德：秘密的激情》。弗洛伊德关于幼儿性欲的演讲激起了群情激愤；会议主席试图恢复秩序时说的那句话——“先生们，我们不是在开政治集会！”——确实把我们引向一个非常有趣的方向：政治与（弗洛伊德式）性理论之间存在一种令人意外的重合。就好像每当性的问题被重新打开，就会有某种政治性的东西被决定。这对精神分析运动自身的政治，以及它在运动内部造成的断裂来说，当然成立。但若在更具体的意义上理解政治，即政治指的是能够围绕某些根本性的社会对抗而被接合起来的东西，那么这一点或许也同样成立。c2_1

When speaking about psychoanalysis and politics today, one usually adopts one of two attitudes. The first is to leave sexuality out, put it aside, and pursue other concepts, such as the (barred) Other, surplus-enjoyment, the Lacanian theory of the four discourses, the Lacanian contribution to the ideology critique. … All of these are of course crucial, yet they cannot be exempted from the issue of the sexual without losing something central, namely, a conceptual articulation of a negativity at work at their core, sustaining them as well as relating them to one another. There is also a second attitude which, in tune with the prevailing (Western) ideology of our time, combines moral liberalism (anything goes, and should be tolerated, as long as there is no abuse involved) with political conservativism (of the status quo, in which every zealous political engagement is by definition “pathological,” unbefitting to “normal,” “non-neurotic” human beings). These two attitudes share a symmetrical (albeit not identical) mistake. The philosophical and politically more radical reading of Lacan dismisses sexuality as something that has only a secondary, anecdotal, or “regional” relevance. And the liberal psychoanalytic reading dismisses politics as something that is necessarily pathological (blind to the impossibility at work in it). The mistake of the first reading is not that it misunderstands the relevance of sexuality, but that it considers it as something (something which simply is, and can be deemed of lesser or greater importance). In the same way, the mistake of the second reading is not that it fails to see that an essentially different politics is nevertheless possible, but again that it takes politics as something, as a fully fledged entity with certain characteristics. In other words, it fails to see that politics is by definition the politics of the impossible (relation). What relates sexuality to politics is that they are not simple ontological categories but essentially imply, depend on, and deploy something which is not of the order of being, and which Lacan refers to as the Real. The Real is precisely not being, but its inherent impasse.

今天谈精神分析与政治时，人们通常采取两种态度之一。第一种是把性排除在外、搁到一边，转而使用其他概念，比如（被划杠的）大他者、剩余享乐、拉康的四种话语理论、拉康对意识形态批判的贡献……这些当然都很关键，但它们不可能绕开性的问题而不丢掉某个核心东西，也就是对其核心处运作的否定性作出概念性表述——这种否定性既支撑它们，也使它们彼此关联。第二种态度则与我们时代主流的（西方）意识形态合拍，把道德自由主义（怎样都行，只要不涉及侵害，就应被容忍）同政治保守主义（维护现状，在其中一切狂热的政治投入按定义都是“病理性的”，不适合“正常的”“非神经症的”人）结合起来。这两种态度共有一个对称的（尽管并不相同的）错误。对拉康作哲学上和政治上更激进的解读，把性贬低为只有次要的、轶事性的或“局部性的”相关性。而自由主义精神分析的解读，则把政治贬为必然病理性的东西（看不到其中运作的不可能性）。第一种解读的错误，不在于它误解了性的相关性，而在于它把性当成一个“东西”（一个单纯存在、可被判定为更重要或不那么重要的东西）。同样，第二种解读的错误，不在于它没看到一种本质上不同的政治仍然可能，而在于它又把政治当成一个东西，当成一个带有某些特征的完整实体。换言之，它看不到：政治按定义就是不可能（关系）的政治。把性与政治联系起来的，是它们并非简单的本体论范畴，而是在根本上蕴含、依赖并展开某种不属于存在秩序的东西，拉康称之为实在界。实在界恰恰不是存在，而是存在自身固有的僵局。

The Lacanian concept of the sexual is not one that provides the best description so far of a certain reality (called sexuality); what it does is develop a unique model of thinking a fundamental non-relationship as dictating the conditions of different kinds of ties (including social ties, or “discourses”). For this is what the Lacanian concept of sexuality is primarily about. It conceptualizes the way in which a fundamental impasse of being is at work in its structuring (as being). It is important, however, to stress the following: by insisting that the Lacanian concept of the sexual is not simply about any kind of sexual content (or sexual practice) we are in no way aiming at its “purification,” trying to produce something like its pure form or pure (philosophical) idea, and hence making it philosophically more acceptable. The point is that beyond all sexual content and practices the sexual is not a pure form, but refers instead to the absence of this form as that which curves and defines the space of the sexual. In other words, this is an “absence” or a negativity that has important consequences for the field structured around it. How do we understand this?

拉康的“性”概念，并不是迄今为止对某种现实（所谓“性”）作出最好描述的概念；它实际做的，是发展出一种独特的思考模型：把一种根本的非关系，设想为规定着不同种类纽带（包括社会纽带，或“话语”）之条件的东西。因为这正是拉康的“性”概念主要处理的问题。它概念化的是：存在的一个根本僵局，如何在（作为存在的）结构化中发挥作用。不过，重要的是要强调下面这一点：我们坚持拉康的“性”概念并不只是关于某种性内容（或性实践），这绝不是要把它“纯化”，也不是要造出某种类似其纯粹形式或纯粹（哲学）观念的东西，从而让它在哲学上更容易被接受。关键在于：在一切性内容和性实践之外，“性”并不是一个纯粹形式，而是指向这种形式的缺席；正是这种缺席弯曲并界定了“性”的空间。换句话说，这是一种“缺席”或否定性，它对围绕它而被结构化的场域有重要后果。我们如何理解这一点？

The paradoxical status of sex is the opposite of, say, the status of unicorns: it is not about an entity that is nowhere to be found empirically, although we know exactly what it would look like if it were found empirically; rather, the opposite: empirically, there is no doubt that sex exists (and we are pretty well able to recognize, “identify” it); what seems to be missing—to put this in Platonic terms—is the Idea of sex, its essence: what exactly do we recognize when we say “this is sex”? Plato went so far as to say that even the lowest things, like mud and dirt, have their corresponding ideas (ideal essences), but what about sex? Is there an Idea, a pure form, of sex? The answer seems to be negative. And this is not because sex would be situated even “lower” on the chain of being than mud or dirt, but for some other reason. Presenting sex as low and “dirty” is already a response, a “solution” to its more fundamental scandal—namely, that we don’t even know what it is. I have already insisted on this point: the embarrassment at and covering up of sexuality, as well as its controlling and regulating, should not be taken as self-explanatory, that is, as explained by the “traditional” cultural ban on sexuality, but rather the other way around: this ban should be explained by an ontological lapse involved in the sexual as sexual. The cause of embarrassment in sexuality is not simply something which is there, on display in it, but on the contrary, something that is not there—something which, if it existed, would determine what sex actually is, and name what is “sexual” about sex. Sex is all around, but we don’t seem to know what exactly it is. We could perhaps go so far as to say: when—in the human realm—we come across something and have absolutely no clue what it is, we can be pretty certain that it “has to do with sex.” This formula is not meant to be ironic. Il n’y a de sexe que de ce qui cloche: there is sex only in something that does not work.

性的悖论地位，和独角兽之类的地位正好相反：问题不在于有一个经验上哪儿都找不到的实体，同时我们又完全知道它若真被找到会像什么；恰恰相反：在经验上，性存在毫无疑问（我们也相当能辨认、 “识别” 它）；看上去缺的——用柏拉图式的话说——是性的理念，是它的本质：当我们说“这就是性”时，到底认出了什么？柏拉图甚至说，连泥巴、脏土这类最低下的东西都有对应的理念（理想本质），那么性呢？有没有一个性的理念、一种纯粹形式？答案似乎是否定的。这并不是因为性在存在链条上比泥巴、脏土还“低”，而是另有原因。把性说成低贱、“肮脏”，本身已经是一种回应，一种对更根本丑闻的“解决”——那丑闻就是：我们根本不知道它是什么。我已经强调过这一点：对性的尴尬与遮盖，以及对性的控制和调节，不应被当作自明之事，也就是说不应由“传统”文化对性的禁令来解释；相反，应该反过来：这道禁令要由性作为性本身所涉及的存在论缺失来解释。性中引起尴尬的原因，并不简单是性中展示出来的某个东西，相反，是某个不在那里的东西——如果它存在，就会决定性实际是什么，并命名性中“性的”东西。性无处不在，但我们似乎不知道它究竟是什么。也许我们甚至可以进一步说：在人类领域，当我们碰到某个东西却完全不知道它是什么时，我们就能相当确定，它“与性有关”。这个公式并不是反讽。Il n’y a de sexe que de ce qui cloche：只有什么东西出故障，才有性。

note：一股子海德格尔重提存在问题的味道（自明、但未知诸如此类）。

In this precise sense culture is not simply a mask/veil of the sexual, it is the mask or, rather, a stand-in for something in the sexual which “is not.” And it is also in this precise (indirect) sense that culture, civilization, is—as the classical Freudian stance goes—sexually driven, “motivated.” It is not driven by that in the sexual which is, but rather by that which is not.

正是在这个意义上，文化并非只是性的面具/面纱；它就是那个面具，或者更准确地说，是性中某个“并不存在”之物的替身。也正是在这个精确而间接的意义上，文化、文明——按经典弗洛伊德立场来说——是由性驱动的，是“有动机的”。驱动它的，不是性中“存在”的东西，而是性中“不存在”的东西。

That in the sexual which is not is the relation: there is no sexual relation. This famous Lacanian claim is often understood too hastily as a learned and clever-sounding formulation of something that people, poems, literature, films have always known and kept repeating in different ways: “(lasting) true love is impossible,” “love is mostly unhappy,” “Men are from Mars, women are from Venus,” “relationships don’t work,” “there are only series of (missed) encounters,” “there are only atomized particles.” … It is easy to show where this kind of understanding moves too quickly and overrides, covers up, the Real expressed by Lacan’s formula. What it does is immediately move to ontologize the non-relation(ship). And so we exclaim: “But of course, there is no sexual relation(ship)! This explains it all (and especially the history of our love life).” The fundamental ontological category, “being as being,” is the non-relation, and this is why we are where we are!

在性之中，那不存在的东西就是关系：不存在性关系。这个著名的拉康命题常常被过于仓促地理解成一种博学而听起来聪明的表述，用来包装人们、诗歌、文学、电影一直知道、并不断以不同方式重复的东西：“（持久的）真爱不可能”，“爱情多半不幸福”，“男人来自火星，女人来自金星”，“关系总是不成”，“只有一连串（错过的）相遇”，“只有原子化的粒子”……很容易指出，这种理解在哪里走得太快，并且压过、掩盖了拉康公式所表达的实在界。它所做的，是立刻把非关系（关系的不存在）本体论化。于是我们喊道：“当然啦，性关系不存在！这就解释了一切（尤其是我们爱情生活的历史）。”基本本体论范畴——“存在之为存在”——就是非关系，而这就是为什么我们成了现在这样！

In this way the non-relation is thus (wrongly) understood as the ultimate truth, the ultimate code or formula of reality.
