The question that will guide us is simply this: how are equivocity and formalization configured in Lacan, and what is the position of truth in this configuration?

然而，在拉康理论的语境中，多义性与形式化（单义性）之间的这种对立或差异，实际上可能是误导性的，甚至干脆是错的。它建立在两种哲学取向的对立与分歧之上；Badiou 用追求真理（以及形式化）与“语言转向”的对立来界定这两种取向。但关键在于看到：从精神分析理论与实践中所引出的语言概念本身，并不是“语言转向”所暗示的那种语言概念；它并不容许这类对立，反而使这种对立站不住脚。至少，这就是我接下来要论证的。我会把 Badiou 和 Cassin 对拉康文本的评注只当作一个起点，不打算呈现他们那复杂且在許多方面极具启发性的论证，也无法充分公正地对待这些论证。引导我们的问题很简单：在拉康那里，多义性与形式化是如何被配置的？真理在这一配置中又处于什么位置？

Let us first briefly return to the quotes where Lacan rejects the imperative nature of interpretation in favor of equivocity. Psychoanalytic intervention and interpretation should not be “theoretical,” “suggestive,” “directive,” “imperative”—what does this mean in fact? It is important first to stress that what is at stake here is not some kind of antitheoretical orientation of psychoanalysis, as analysts sometimes like to understand from such claims—what Lacan critiques here is not psychoanalytic theory, but the kind of practice which brings theory into play at the wrong point or in the wrong way. Let us hear the words from Lacan himself, who this time spells out very clearly what is at stake:

If there is a principal law of psychoanalysis, it is that we shouldn’t blather, not even in the name of analytical categories. No wild analysis: one shouldn’t be using words that make sense only for the analyst. I learn everything from my analysands; it is from them that I learn what psychoanalysis is about. I borrow my interventions from them, and not from my teaching—except if I know that they know exactly what something means. I replaced the word “word” by the word “signifier”; and this means that it lends itself to equivocity, to several possible meanings. And if you choose your words well—the words that will haunt the analysand—you will find the elected signifier, the one which will work. (Lacan 1976, 34)

让我们先简短地回到那些引文：拉康在其中拒斥解释的命令性质，转而拥护多义性。精神分析的干预与解释不应是“理论性的”“暗示性的”“指导性的”“命令性的”——这实际上意味着什么？首先要强调，这里涉及的并不是精神分析的某种反理论取向，分析家有时喜欢从这类说法里听出这个意思——拉康在此批评的不是精神分析理论，而是那种在错误时点或以错误方式让理论登场的实践。让我们听听拉康自己的话，这次他非常清楚地说明了问题所在：

如果精神分析有一条首要法则，那就是我们不该胡扯，哪怕打着分析范畴的旗号也不行。不要野蛮分析：不该使用只对分析家有意义的词。我从我的分析者那里学到一切；正是从他们那里，我学到精神分析到底在讲什么。我的干预是从他们那里借来的，不是从我的教学里借来的——除非我知道他们确切地知道某个东西意味着什么。我把“词”这个词换成了“能指”；这意味着它允许多义，允许好几种可能的含义。如果你选词选得好——选那些会萦绕分析者的词——你就会找到那个被选中的能指，那个能起作用的能指。（Lacan 1976, 34）

It is not out of (false or sincere) modesty that Lacan says “I learn everything from my analysands,” “I borrow my interventions from them.” Rather, this is a procedure, a method that is carefully thought out, and actually recalls Hegel’s warning, in the Preface to the Phenomenology of Spirit, against the kind of (philosophical) proceeding which concerns itself only with aims and results, with differentiating and passing judgments on things. This kind of activity, says Hegel, instead of getting involved with the thing, is always-already beyond it; instead of tarrying with it, and being preoccupied with it, this kind of knowing remains essentially preoccupied with itself (Hegel 1977, 3). The proximity of “practicing analyst” Lacan and “speculative philosopher” Hegel on these questions of method should be enough to prevent any hasty conclusions drawn in terms of theory versus practice, philosophy versus antiphilosophy, or singular versus universal.

拉康说“我从我的分析者那里学到一切”，“我从他们那里借用我的干预”，这并不是出于（虚假或真诚的）谦虚。相反，这是一种经过仔细思考的程序、一种方法，而且实际上让人想起黑格尔在《精神现象学》序言中的警告：要警惕那种只关心目的和结果、只对事物作区分和判断的（哲学）做法。黑格尔说，这种活动不是介入事情本身，而是总已经超出它；不是与它逗留、专注于它，这种认识本质上仍然专注于自身（Hegel 1977, 3）。“实践分析家”拉康和“思辨哲学家”黑格尔在方法问题上的这种相近，足以防止任何草率结论——无论这些结论是以理论对实践、哲学对反哲学，还是单一对普遍的名义作出的。

A psychoanalyst is not an expert treating patients with her expertise, which she would apply to symptoms of a given concrete case. If one wants to shift something in the thing (in the unconscious structure), one has to allow it to speak, for it alone can come up with, produce, the word that eventually “works,” moves things. But one—in this case the analyst—should of course be able to recognize the “right word.” And this is not simply a practical (clinical) stance, but also a theoretical one.

分析家不是专家：不是用自己的专业知识去治疗病人，再把这套知识套到某个具体个案的症状上。若想在事物（无意识结构）中挪动点什么，就得让它说话，因为只有它自己才能找到、生产出最终“起作用”、推动事物的那个词。但一个人——在这里就是分析家——当然应该能认出那个“对的词”。这不只是实践（临床）立场，也是理论立场。

To return to our central question: what does this emphasis on the equivocal imply in relation to the also clearly stated ideal of formalization, which is very much present in Lacan’s work?

回到我们的核心问题：这种对歧义的强调，相对于同样被明确表述的形式化理想——它在拉康的著作中十分突出——究竟意味着什么？

The answer may be surprising in its very simplicity: equivocity itself can function directly as a formula, as is clear already in the example of “Je te m’athème,” as well as, for instance, in how equivocal punch lines function in jokes. In the case of jokes, playing on equivocity, and introducing another meaning with its help, does not have the result of relativizing meaning; it is, rather, designed so that we get a very precise, isolated point. Let me repeat here a joke that I used in my book on comedy. It is worth repeating not only because it illustrates this point perfectly, but also because we will be able to reconfigure it and use it further on as an example of possible analytic intervention.

A man comes home from an exhausting day at work, plops down on the couch in front of the television, and tells his wife: “Get me a beer before it starts.”
The wife sighs and gets him a beer. Fifteen minutes later, he says: “Get me another beer before it starts.” She looks cross, but fetches another beer and slams it down next to him. He finishes that beer and a few minutes later says:
“Quick, get me another beer, it’s going to start any minute.” The wife is furious.
She yells at him: “Is that all you’re going to do tonight? Drink beer and sit in front of that TV? You’re nothing but a lazy, drunken, fat slob, and furthermore …”
The man sighs, and says: “It’s started …”

答案可能简单得令人意外：双关性本身就能直接充当公式；从“Je te m’athème”这个例子已经能看清这一点，也比如在笑话里，双关的包袱如何起作用。就笑话而言，玩弄双关性、借它引入另一个意义，并不会让意义变得相对；相反，它是被设计来让我们得到一个非常精确、孤立的点。这里我再讲一个我在论喜剧的书里用过的笑话。它值得重复，不仅因为它完美地说明了这一点，也因为我们之后能重新配置它，把它当作分析性干预之可能性的例子来用。

一个男人下班回家，精疲力尽，一屁股坐到电视机前的沙发上，对妻子说：“趁它还没开始，给我拿杯啤酒。”
妻子叹了口气，给他拿了杯啤酒。十五分钟后，他说：“趁它还没开始，再给我拿一杯。”她面露愠色，但还是拿了另一杯，重重地放在他旁边。他喝完那杯，几分钟后又说：“快点，再给我拿杯啤酒，它随时就要开始了。”妻子暴怒。
她冲他吼道：“你今晚就打算干这个？喝啤酒，坐在电视前面？你就是个又懒又醉又胖的邋遢鬼，而且……”
男人叹了口气，说：“它开始了……”

The final punch line is equivocal, yet the point it transmits is not. This kind of equivocal punch line does not open up a multiplicity of possible meanings; rather, it evokes and uses this multiplicity in order to efficiently pin down and transmit a singular point (or impasse); and to transmit it in a most economical way—not by fully and exhaustively describing it, but by directly naming it: that is, precisely, so that it functions somewhat like a formula. It is of course true that such “formulas” are “universally transmittable” only if one speaks the language “of which” they are made. Unlike mathematical formulas they depend on living languages, on people speaking them. But this is not so much a limitation as precisely that which makes it possible for these equi­vocities to work as formulas, and to work tout court: to “make waves,” to “bore a hole”—to have consequences for the reality which they formalize.

最后那句包袱是含混的，但它所传递的那个点并不含混。这种含混的包袱并不打开可能意义的多重性；相反，它唤起并利用这种多重性，只为高效地钉住并传递一个单一的点（或僵局）；并且以最经济的方式传递它——不是完整而详尽地描述它，而是直接给它命名：也就是说，恰恰让它多少像公式一样运作。当然，这类“公式”只有在人们说“构成它们的”那门语言时，才是“可普遍传递的”。与数学公式不同，它们依赖活生生的语言，依赖说这些语言的人。但这与其说是限制，不如说正是这一点使这些含混能够作为公式起作用，并且干脆说，能够起作用：能“掀起波澜”、能“钻孔”——能对它们所形式化的现实产生后果。

What is a symptom that one “brings” to analysis? It is always a subjective solution to some contradiction or impasse. And it is a solution that usually makes one’s life very complicated; it comes with some degree of suffering. Yet it is a solution, and it involves serious subjective investment. The work of analysis consists in forcing out the contradiction “solved” by the symptom, in relating the symptom to the singular contradiction of which it is a solution. Psychoanalysis does not solve the contradiction; rather, it solves its solution (given by the symptom). It bores a hole where the symptom has built a dense net of significations. And the subject needs to “reconstruct” herself as part of this contradiction, as directly implied in it. (To be sure, this does not mean that here we are on the level of the particular/individual, as opposed to the general or communal. The contradiction that affects an individual is intrinsically social—others, and our relation to them, as well as social relations more generally, are already implied in it.)

一个人“带到”分析里的症状是什么？它总是对某个矛盾或僵局的主体解决办法。并且这种解决办法通常会让人的生活变得非常复杂，还伴随一定程度的痛苦。但它仍然是一种解决，而且包含严肃的主体投注。分析的工作，就是逼出症状所“解决”的那个矛盾，把症状与它所解决的那个独一矛盾联系起来。精神分析不解决矛盾；它解决的是矛盾的那个解决（由症状给出的解决）。它在症状已经织起密集意义网络的地方钻一个洞。主体则需要把自己“重构”为这个矛盾的一部分，直接卷入其中。（当然，这并不意味着这里我们处在特殊/个体的层面，而与普遍/共同体层面相对。影响个体的矛盾本质上是社会的——他人、我们与他人的关系，以及更一般的社会关系，已经内含于其中。）

The right (equivocal) word “bores a hole” because it repeats/names the enjoyment that holds (“glues”) different meanings together in a symptomatic way. It brings out the negativity (contradiction) which is shared, repeated, and obfuscated by the deployment of these different meanings, and it does so by spoiling/dissolving the enjoyment (surplus excitation) that emerges at the place of this negativity or contradiction (and associates these different meanings). The right word is not the right word by virtue of what it means, but by virtue of what it accomplishes.

恰当（含混）的词之所以“钻出一个洞”，是因为它重复/命名了那种以症状的方式把不同意义粘在一起的享乐。它带出被这些不同意义的部署所共享、重复和遮蔽的否定性（矛盾）；而它这样做，靠的是败坏/消解在那个否定性或矛盾的位置上涌现出来的享乐（剩余兴奋），也正是这种享乐把这些不同意义关联起来。恰当的词之所以恰当，不在于它意味着什么，而在于它做成了什么。

What is at stake here is thus perhaps not best staged in terms of the relationship (or opposition) between equivocity and univocity. As Lacan himself suggests, univocity is, rather, a characteristic of the symbols of animal language.

All that appears-is (parest) in [language] of a semblant of communication is always dream, lapsus, or joke. Nothing to do then with what is imagined or confirmed in many points of animal language. The real there is not to be distanced from a univocal communication, from which the animals as well, in giving us the model, would make us their dolphins: a function of code exercises itself in it. … Even more, some vital conducts organize themselves there with symbols in every respect similar to ours (erection of an object to the rank of a master-signifier in the order of the flight of migration, symbolism of the parade as often amorous as of combat, signals of labor, marks of territory), except that these symbols are never equivocal. (Lacan 1973, 47)

因此，这里的关键也许最好不要放在多义性与单义性之间的关系（或对立）中来展开。正如拉康本人所提示的，单义性反倒是动物语言符号的一个特征。

在交流之假相的［语言］中，一切显现-存在（parest）都总是梦、口误或玩笑。因此，这与动物语言在诸多点位上被想象或被确认的东西毫无关系。那里的实在界并不远离一种单义交流；动物在给我们提供模型时，也会借此把我们变成它们的海豚：一种代码的功能就在其中运作。……更进一步说，某些生命行为在那里借助与我们各方面都相似的象征来组织自身（在迁徙飞行的秩序中，把对象提升至主人能指的地位；炫耀的象征化，它常常既关乎爱欲也关乎争斗；劳作信号；领地标记），只不过这些象征从来不是含混的。

Equivocity, on the other hand, is the very inherent condition of formalization, insofar as the formalization is not to be confused with symbols and codes as they seem to function between animals. In psychoanalysis, formalization is not a formalization of this or that content, of this or that meaning (as “the right meaning”)—it is the formalization of the very impasse/“hole” through which (and only through which) these meanings exist as bound together in a given configuration. Free associations produce piles of associated meanings. And the right word is the key to the twisted logic of this association. How do we know we have the right key? Because it works—it works in the direction of disassociation. The key in psychoanalysis is not simply a hermeneutical key, although hermeneutics is also important.

歧义性，另一方面，正是形式化本身固有的条件，只要形式化不该和象征与代码混为一谈——后者在动物之间似乎就是这样运作的。在精神分析中，形式化不是对某种内容、某种意义（比如“正确的意义”）的形式化；它是对那个僵局/“洞”本身的形式化，正是通过这个僵局/“洞”（而且只有通过它），这些意义才在某个既定构型中作为彼此联结在一起的东西而存在。自由联想会产生一堆堆相互关联的意义。而那个对的词，就是这种联想之扭曲逻辑的钥匙。我们怎么知道自己拿对了钥匙？因为它管用——它是在解除联想的方向上管用。精神分析中的钥匙并不只是一个解释学的钥匙，尽管解释学也很重要。

As Slavoj Žižek formulated this in relation to the Freudian theory of dreams and to his thesis that “dreams are nothing other than a particular form of thinking” (Freud 1988, 650):

First we must break the appearance according to which a dream is nothing but a simple and meaningless confusion, a disorder caused by physiological processes and as such having nothing whatsoever to do with signification. In other words, we must accomplish a crucial step towards a hermeneutical approach and conceive the dream as a meaningful phenomenon, as something transmitting a repressed message which has to be discovered by an interpretative procedure.
Then we must get rid of the fascination in this kernel of signification, in the “hidden meaning” of the dream—that is to say, in the content concealed behind the form of a dream—and center our attention on this form itself, on the dream-work to which the “latent dream-thoughts” were submitted. (Žižek 1989, 14)

正如斯拉沃热·齐泽克在联系弗洛伊德的梦理论，以及弗洛伊德“梦无非是一种特殊形式的思维”这一命题时所作出的表述：

首先，我们必须打破这样一种表象：梦不过是简单而无意义的混乱，是由生理过程造成的一种失序，因此与意指毫无干系。换句话说，我们必须迈出通向诠释学进路的关键一步，把梦设想为一种有意义的现象，设想为某种传递被压抑讯息的东西，而这个讯息必须通过解释程序才能被发现。
然后，我们必须摆脱对意指内核的迷恋，摆脱对梦的“隐藏意义”的迷恋——也就是说，摆脱对隐藏在梦的形式背后的内容的迷恋——并把注意力集中到这个形式本身，集中到潜隐梦思所经受的梦的工作上。（Žižek 1989, 14）

The unconscious desire is not the content of the hidden message, it is the active designer of the form that latent thoughts get in a dream.

无意识欲望不是隐藏信息的内容，而是潜隐思想在梦中所获形式的主动设计者。

This is why the key in psychoanalysis is not a key to a hidden meaning, but the key that “unlocks” this form itself (makes what has been associated to compose the hidden meaning dissociate). And this is what “the right word” does.

这就是为什么，精神分析里的钥匙，不是一把通向隐藏意义的钥匙，而是那把“打开”这个形式本身的钥匙（让那些经由联想而组合成隐藏意义的元素彼此解离）。而“恰当的词”所做的，正是这件事。

We could actually reconfigure the “It’s started …” joke as something that constitutes a possible psychoanalytic intervention. A patient is repeatedly complaining to his analyst how, when he comes home from work, all he wants is to lie on the couch, drink beer, and watch his favorite TV show. He insists, repeatedly, on how much he likes to have a few beers before the show starts, which is why he has the habit of asking his wife just this: to bring him a beer or two before it starts. Yet inevitably, he complains, his wife explodes and starts shouting insults at him.—“And so it starts,” intervenes the analyst. The point of this intervention is not simply that the true hidden meaning behind the husband’s repeating of the words “before it starts” is a reference to frequent domestic quarrels, but also to shift the focus to the form itself: this whole staging (the whole scene as acted out with his wife) IS his favorite show. And this point is made not simply so that the husband will understand what he is really saying, but also to spoil for him the symptomatic enjoyment invested in this scene of the domestic quarrel and its anticipation. In this precise sense we could say that the form of the symptom (the specific work of the unconscious) is “unlocked” by this intervention.

我们其实可以把“它开始了……”这个笑话重新配置成一种可能的精神分析干预。一个病人不断向他的分析家抱怨：下班回家后，他想要的就是躺在沙发上、喝啤酒、看自己最喜欢的电视节目。他反复强调自己多么喜欢在节目开始前喝上几瓶啤酒，所以他习惯只要求妻子做一件事：在节目开始前给他拿一两瓶啤酒。然而，他抱怨说，妻子每次都不可避免地爆发，开始对他破口大骂。——“于是它开始了，”分析家插话说。这个干预的要点不只是：丈夫反复说“在它开始前”这几个字，背后真正隐藏的含义是在指频繁的家庭争吵；同时还要把焦点转到形式本身：这一整套搬演（与妻子一起上演的整场戏）就是他的最爱节目。而且，点出这一点，不只是为了让丈夫明白他真正在说什么，也是为了搅坏他投注在家庭争吵场景及其预期中的症状性享乐。正是在这个确切意义上，我们可以说，症状的形式（无意识的特定工作）被这一干预“解锁”了。

The psychoanalytic key could thus be described precisely as fitting the point where “truth holds onto the real,” to use Lacan’s wording from Television: “I always speak the truth. Not the whole truth, because there is no way to say it all. Saying it all is materially impossible: words fail. Yet it is through this very impossibility that the truth holds onto the real” (Lacan 1990, 3). And this point where “truth holds onto the real” is precisely the point involved in formalization. Formalization is not a truth about the Real, but concerns the point of entanglement of speech (as sustaining the dimension of truth) with the Real.

因此，精神分析的钥匙或许可以准确地被描述为：它正好对准“真理紧抓住实在界”的那个点——借用拉康在《电视》中的说法：“我始终说真理。不是全部真理，因为没有办法把一切都说出来。把一切说出来在物质上是不可能的：词语会失效。然而，正是通过这种不可能性，真理才紧抓住实在界”（Lacan 1990, 3）。而“真理紧抓住实在界”的这个点，正是形式化所涉及的点。形式化并不是关于实在界的一种真理，而是关涉到言语（作为支撑真理维度者）与实在界相纠缠的那个点。

And here we come to the core of the difference between Lacan and Badiou, as Badiou sees it: what makes Lacan an antiphilosopher (or sophist) is his claim that we cannot speak about the Real (and that there is no truth about the real), and that the Real does not allow for metalanguage. However, on the basis of what has been said so far, we can already see the crucial difference that separates Lacan from, for example, the Wittgensteinian version of this claim. We cannot speak about the Real because speech is too close to it, because it can never fully escape the Real, but holds onto it. This is why, instead of the prohibition of the impossible, which we find in Wittgenstein (“whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent”—the famous lines from his Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus), we have in Lacan its double reversal: go on, speak about anything whatsoever, and with a little luck and help (from the analyst) you will sooner or later stumble against the Real, and get to formalize (write) it. The Real is not some realm or substance to be talked about, it is the inherent contradiction of speech, twisting its tongue, so to speak. And this is precisely why there is truth, and why, at the same time, it is not possible to say it all.

… one needs to accept that we speak of truth as a fundamental position, even though we don’t know it all, since I define it with the fact that it is only possible to half say it. (Lacan 2011, 173)

在 Badiou 看来，拉康与他本人的差异，核心正在这里：使拉康成为反哲学家（或智者）的，是他主张我们不能谈论实在界（也不存在关于实在界的真理）3_16，并且实在界不允许元语言。不过，基于前文，我们已经能看出一个关键差异：拉康不同于例如维特根斯坦对这一主张的表述。我们不能谈论实在界，是因为言语离它太近，因为言语永远无法完全摆脱实在界，反而会黏着它。所以，维特根斯坦那里有对不可能之物的禁令（“凡不可说者，必须沉默”——他《逻辑哲学论》中的名句），拉康这里则是这一禁令的双重反转：继续说下去，随便说什么都行；只要有点运气，再得到（分析家的）帮助，你迟早会撞上实在界，并得以把它形式化（书写）出来。实在界不是某个可供谈论的领域或实体，它是言语内在的矛盾，可以说，它让言语的舌头打结。正因如此，才有真理；也正因如此，才不可能把它全部说出来。

……必须接受，我们把真理作为一个根本立场来谈论，即便我们并不全知道它，因为我正是根据“它只能被半说”这一事实来定义它。（Lacan 2011, 173）

This is a quote from 1972, testifying to the fact that truth remains a central category also in “late” Lacan. And, as I have also pointed out, it is through this impossibility of saying it all that the truth holds onto the Real. Paradox is another indicator of this impossibility; it indicates that language cannot be neatly separated from the Real (about which it would supposedly speak). And the place/position of truth is the point where speech “slips,” “lapses” into the Real that it tries to articulate. It is not simply that this Real can be “felt” (experienced or “monstrated”) only as the limit of the discourse—it is possible to formalize it. This is what Lacanian mathemes are all about. A matheme is not simply a formalization of some reality; rather—and as Lacan himself puts it—it is the formalization of the impasse of formalization.

这是1972年的一段引文，它证明：在“晚期”拉康那里，真理仍然是一个核心范畴。而且，正如我也指出的，正是通过这种不能全都说出来的不可能性，真理才依附于实在界。悖论是这种不可能性的另一个标志；它表明语言无法与实在界干干净净地分开（人们本以为语言说的就是实在界）。而真理的位置/场所，就是言语“滑开”、“失足”落入它试图言说的实在界的那个点。并不是说这个实在界只能作为话语的界限被“感到”（被经验或被“展示”）——它也是可以被形式化的。这正是拉康派数学素的关键所在。数学素并不只是对某种现实的形式化；更确切地说——正如拉康本人所说——它是对形式化之僵局的形式化。

What is thus crucially important to point out is that for Lacan, it is not simply formalization as such that is interesting. What is interesting are the impasses (paradoxes) it produces—as points of its own impossibility which can themselves be “formalized.” And this is precisely why logic (and especially modern mathematical logic) is such an important reference for him: because it makes this double move possible.

This examination of the logics is not simply interrogation of that which sets its limit to the speech in its apprehension of the Real. In the very structure of this effort to approach the Real, in the very handling of this structure, the latter shows that which of the Real has determined the speech. (Lacan 2011, 20)

因此，关键要指出的是：对拉康来说，有趣的并不只是形式化本身。真正有趣的是形式化所产生的僵局（悖论）——作为形式化自身不可能性的点，而这些点本身还可以被“形式化”。正因如此，逻辑学（尤其是现代数理逻辑）对他而言才是如此重要的参照：因为它使这种双重操作成为可能。

这种对诸逻辑的考察，并不只是去质询：在言语把握实在界时，是什么给言语设定了界限。在接近实在界的这一努力本身的结构中，在对这一结构本身的处理中，该结构就显示出：实在界的什么东西决定了言语。（Lacan 2011, 20）

It would be difficult to put it more precisely: the structure that attempts to articulate the Real is determined in its foundation by the Real it attempts to formulate. Yet this very determination, far from discrediting in advance all approaches to the Real by way of the structure, is precisely what can make them credible. It is this very determination that can eventually ground (or justify) the psychoanalytic claims to realism. This grounding of realism can take place only from a certain folding of the structure upon itself, and from a singular perspective (“looking awry”) on this folding. Psychoanalysis is what introduces this singular perspective.

很难说得更精确了：那个试图表述实在界的结构，在其根基上是由它试图表述的实在界所决定的。然而，这种决定关系非但不会预先取消一切经由结构接近实在界的路径，反而恰恰能让这些路径变得可信。正是这种决定关系，最终能够为精神分析关于实在论的主张奠基（或证明其正当性）。3_17 这种对实在论的奠基，只能从结构对自身的某种折叠中、并从针对这一折叠的一种独特视角（“斜目而视”）出发才可能发生。精神分析正是引入这一独特视角的东西。

From its very beginning, logic has advanced by articulating paradoxes. Lacan reminds us of this in “L’étourdit”: “I will simply recall that no logical development, this starting before Socrates and from elsewhere than in our tradition, ever proceeded except from a kernel of paradoxes” (Lacan 1973, 49). Yet he does not consider these paradoxes as the limit of rationalist endeavors, as proof of how the Real is inaccessible to discourse, or as proof of the uselessness and arbitrariness of the concept of truth; on the contrary, he takes them as that which can ground rationality, what testifies to the irreducible link between the discursive and the Real, and that which alone opens the space of truth as a fundamental position.

从一开始，逻辑学就是通过把悖论表述出来而向前推进的。拉康在《L’étourdit》中提醒我们这一点：“我只想提醒一句：任何逻辑发展——它始于苏格拉底之前，并且来自我们传统之外的别处——都只有从一个悖论的内核出发才得以展开”（Lacan 1973, 49）。然而，他并不把这些悖论看作理性主义努力的界限，不看作实在界无法被话语通达的证明，也不看作真理概念无用而任意的证明；相反，他把它们看作能够为理性奠定基础的东西，看作证明话语与实在界之间存在不可化约之联系的东西，并且看作唯有它们才开启真理作为根本立场之空间的东西。

Psychoanalysis does also not simply “side with” the scientific advances made by solving these paradoxes, but accompanies them by producing a singular perspective on them; it formalizes what makes these advances necessary, it formalizes the impasses of formalization. This is why “analytical discourse is not scientific discourse, it is a discourse to which science provides its material, and that is something considerably different” (Lacan 2011, 141).

精神分析也并不只是简单地“站在”那些通过解决这些悖论而取得的科学进步一边，而是伴随着这些进步，并对它们给出一种独特的视角；它把使这些进步成为必要的东西形式化，也把形式化自身的僵局形式化。正因如此，“分析话语不是科学话语，它是科学为其提供材料的话语，而这是相当不同的另一回事”（Lacan 2011, 141）。

The specificity of the Lacanian notion of formalization, which is not simply scientific formalization, should also be pointed out in response to a criticism of his method that one sometimes hears: if formalization actually writes something that cannot be said (better) in any other way, are the eventual explanations of these formulas and schemes, then, utterly misguided? If that which is “said” by a formula can be verbalized without loss, unraveled into simple prose, why use formulas at all? If, however, it is not possible to do this, then all “translations” of formulas into prose introduce, conceptually speaking, an irreversible loss.

还应指出拉康式形式化概念的特殊性——它并不只是科学意义上的形式化——以此回应人们有时听到的、对其方法的一种批评：如果形式化实际上写下了某种无法以任何其他方式（或更好地）说出的东西，那么后来对这些公式和图式所作的解释，难道就完全是误入歧途吗？如果公式所“说”的东西能够无损地用言语表达出来，能够拆解成简单平白的文字，那为什么还要用公式？可是，如果这件事做不到，那么所有把公式“翻译”成平白文字的做法，从概念上讲，都会引入一种不可逆的损失。

Just as in the case of the relationship between equivocity and formalization, the answer is that we should not oppose formulas and verbalization. Lacan does not resort to formulas in order to avoid the ambiguities of everyday speech and make sure that there is only one possible meaning to them, but sees the formulas as related to the very logic (and dialectic) of the verbal, insofar as the verbal is inherently inseparable from the negativity that generates it. That is why we can gain as much (or even more) by verbalization of the formal as by a formalization of the impasses of the verbal. There are legendary stories circulating about how Lacan, in his last seminars, almost stopped talking—instead he would make some of his famous knots, and simply throw them to the audience. This indeed conveys the image of a wise man who lost all confidence in words and opted for a “higher” and more reliable mode of communication. Or else, as suggested by Jean-Claude Milner, it conveys the image of a man who relied on topology as a means of destroying language. Yet both these images are somehow at odds with the following explicit statement, also belonging to the repertoire of Lacan’s late work (1975): “I do not use knots because they are non-verbal. On the contrary, I try to verbalize them” (Lacan 1976, 35). True, this verbalization could itself be seen as a destruction of language, or as a means of its destruction—or as a pathway to a fundamentally different kind of language. Lacan’s late hommage to Joyce could be seen as going in this direction. Yet I share the conviction of many of my friends (starting with Badiou) that Beckett is a more compelling, and probably more Lacanian, author than Joyce. We can get a much better idea of what “verbalizing the knots” may amount to by reading Beckett than by reading Joyce. We may also get a better idea of how “boring holes” in language relates to equivocity. Commenting on Beckett’s decision to write in French instead of in his mother tongue, Mladen Dolar wrote:

正如在歧义性与形式化之间的关系这一情形中一样，答案是我们不应把公式与言语化对立起来。拉康诉诸公式，并不是为了避免日常言语的歧义、确保它们只有一种可能含义，而是把公式看作与言语本身的逻辑（以及辩证法）相关，因为言语内在地离不开产生它的否定性。这就是为什么，把形式的东西言语化，和把言语的僵局形式化，能让我们获得同样多（甚至更多）的东西。有一些传奇故事流传：拉康在最后的研讨班上几乎不再讲话——相反，他会打一些他著名的结，然后直接扔给听众。这确实传达出一个智者的形象：他对词语失去了全部信心，转而选择一种“更高”也更可靠的交流方式。或者，如Jean-Claude Milner所提示的3_18，它传达出这样一个人的形象：他依靠拓扑学作为摧毁语言的手段。然而，这两种形象都与下面这个明确说法有些相抵触，而它同样属于拉康晚期作品（1975）的一部分：“我使用结，并不是因为它们是非言语的。相反，我试图把它们言语化”（Lacan 1976, 35）。确实，这种言语化本身可以被看作对语言的摧毁，或看作摧毁语言的手段——或看作通往一种根本不同的语言的道路。拉康晚期对Joyce的致敬可以被看作朝这个方向走。然而，我和许多朋友（从Badiou开始）抱有同样的确信：Beckett是比Joyce更有说服力、也可能更拉康派的作者。读Beckett比读Joyce更能让我们了解“把结言语化”可能意味着什么。我们也能更好地了解“在语言中打洞”如何与歧义性相关。在评论Beckett决定用法语而不是母语写作时，Mladen Dolar写道：

The first decision that followed from the revelation was to start writing in French and thus to escape the “Anglo-Irish exuberance and automatisms,” but this involved a lot more: to escape the tentacles of the mother tongue as the seemingly natural home ground of self-expression, of one’s cultural heritage, the territory of the spontaneous and the homely. The mother tongue is not an ally but a foe. But this only leads to this wider claim: language is not an ally but a foe. To write in a language where one is not at home is just a consequence of the fact that one is never at home in a language, so that mother tongue, and ultimately language as such, is but a refuge against what literature ought to do. To bore holes in language, as he famously put it in the letter to Axel Kaun.

那次启示之后作出的第一个决定，就是开始用法语写作，从而逃离“盎格鲁-爱尔兰式的丰溢与自动机制”；但这牵涉的远不止于此：要逃离母语的触手——母语看似是自我表达的自然家园，是文化传承的自然家园，是自发之物与熟悉之物的领地。母语不是盟友，而是敌人。但这只引出一个更宽的论断：语言不是盟友，而是敌人。用一门自己并不安身其中的语言写作，不过是一个事实的后果：人从来就不在任何语言中安身；因此母语，最终说语言本身，不过是用来躲避文学本应去做之事的避难所。在语言中钻孔，正如他在给 Axel Kaun 的信中那句名言所说。3_19

As we have seen, the Beckettian formulation “to bore holes in language” does indeed resonate strongly with, and in, (the late work of) Lacan. It is also directly related to the issue of equivocity: Dolar also reminds us how, in a wonderful pun, Beckett once said: “En français on est si mal armé”: one is so poorly equipped in French as a foreign speaker, but there is Mallarmé lurking behind even the simplest expressions.

正如我们所见，Beckett式的说法“在语言中打洞”，确实与拉康（晚期工作）强烈共鸣，也在拉康那里回响。它还直接关系到歧义性问题：Dolar 也提醒我们，Beckett曾用一个绝妙双关说过：“En français on est si mal armé”：作为外语者，人在法语中如此“装备不良”，可即便最简单的表达背后，也潜伏着 Mallarmé。

This is indeed a perfect example of equivocity that can function as a formula. It could even be considered as the formula of the very inseparability of equi­vocity and formalization. This Beckettian pun is also exactly of the kind used, or practiced, by Lacan. For there are puns and puns, and there are different ways of “using” them. Lacan tends to use them as “formulas,” and there is actually a very interesting proximity between his and Beckett’s usage of puns.

这确实是一个绝佳例子，说明“多义性”可以充当公式。甚至可以把它看作多义性与形式化本身不可分离的公式。这个Beckett式双关，也恰恰是拉康所使用、或所实践的那类双关。因为双关也分很多种，而“使用”双关的方式也各不相同。拉康倾向于把双关当作“公式”来用；实际上，他使用双关的方式和Beckett使用双关的方式之间，有一种非常有趣的接近性。

Earlier on I suggested that psychoanalysis does not resolve the contradiction but, rather, its (symptomatic) solution. It is now time to ask if this is simply a kind of practical bottom line of psychoanalysis? That is: is there a fundamental (structural) impossibility or contradiction which is irreducible, and all we can do is circumscribe or recognize it as such and accept it, in order to prevent it from secretly feeding our pathological fantasies? This is indeed the bottom line in a certain (“liberal”) understanding of psychoanalysis. However, I do not think one should endorse this perspective, but rather insist that, no, this is not the bottom line. On the contrary: it is not about accepting the contradiction, but about taking one’s place in it. This is what the “position of truth” could be understood to amount to. (And let us not forget that in analysis, formalization relates to a shift in our position—otherwise it does not work, and is not “the right formalization,” as Lacan also puts it.) Yet this shift of perspective does not occur in the movement that would go from the surface toward the foundation (contradiction as foundation). Rather, the foundation appears (takes place) as a splitting of the surface itself. That is to say: the fundamental contradiction appears to be inherent to the terms involved in it. This, for example, is precisely what the Lacanian formulas of sexuation force us to think: not the contradiction between “opposite” sexes, but the contradiction inherent to both, “barring” them both from within.

前面我提过，精神分析并不解决矛盾，而倒是矛盾的（症状性）解决。现在该问：这是否只是精神分析的一种实践底线？也就是说：是否存在一种根本的（结构性的）不可能性或矛盾，它是不可化约的，而我们所能做的只是圈定它、承认它如此并接受它，以免它暗中滋养我们的病理性幻想？在某种（“自由主义式”）的精神分析理解里，这确实是底线。不过，我不认为应该赞同这种视角，反而要坚持：不，这不是底线。恰恰相反：问题不在于接受矛盾，而在于在矛盾中占据自己的位置。所谓“真理的位置”，或许正可作此理解。（也别忘记，在分析中，形式化关系到我们位置的转变——否则它就不起作用，也不是拉康所说的“对的形式化”。）然而，这种视角的转变并不是发生在从表面走向基础（把矛盾当作基础）的运动中。相反，基础是作为表面自身的分裂而显现（发生）的。也就是说：根本矛盾似乎内在于它所涉及的各项之中。例如，拉康的性化公式恰恰迫使我们思考的正是这一点：不是“对立”两性之间的矛盾，而是二者各自固有的矛盾，从内部把二者都“划杠”。

note：男性一边维护规则，一边又认同“例外”而痛苦；女人一边被象征化，另一边又总是无法闭合而无法获得“完全认同”而痛苦。

When I talk about a “fundamental contradiction” I am not referring to some contradiction buried deep down in the foundation of things, and influencing them from there. Contradiction is “fundamental” in the sense that it is persistent, and repeating—yet always in concrete situations, on the surface of things and in the present. It is by engaging with it in these concrete situations that we work with the “fundamental contradiction.”

当我谈“根本矛盾”时，我指的不是某种埋在事物根基深处、并从那里影响事物的矛盾。说矛盾是“根本的”，意思是它持续存在、不断重复——但总是出现在具体情境中，在事物的表面上，在当下。正是在这些具体情境中与它打交道，我们才是在和“根本矛盾”一起工作。

Contradiction is not something that we simply have to accept and “make do with”; it can become, and be “used” as, the source of emancipation from the very logic dictated by this contradiction. This is what analysis ideally leads to: contradiction does not simply disappear, but the way it functions in the discourse structuring our reality changes radically. And this happens as a result of our fully and actively engaging in the contradiction, taking our place in it.
